With the codes it reads the code-aware one-per-bill lists (`C528`'s linkage included); without them, the flag list per entry. *(And `perEntry`'s expanded-row sentinel is the numeric line suffix `_\d+$` now, not any underscore — `C288`'s recorded fixture collision, met on `ap_null`. And the legacy no-tools snapshot's *"overdue receivables"* takes the A/R code now, so a direct deposit is not "past due" there either.)*
- **C531 — THE MATCHER'S "NOT THE BANK LINES THEMSELVES" GUARD NAMED ONLY THE IN-SESSION SOURCE SPELLINGS (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** A census of every `source` written against every `source` compared: `matchableOpenItems` excluded `bank_feed` and `bank_statement`, and the RPC stores every bank line as `bank_import` (`normalizeSource`), so after a reload the guard matched nothing — it held only by virtue of `isSettlementEntry` (`C261`'s shape). A payment booked from the bank rail WITHOUT a `payment_for` link — a legacy line, or one the matcher could not pair — carries an A/P leg and was offered as an open payable to match the next month's debit against: the O83 Feb bug's shape through the one door that guard was written for. The stored name is excluded too. *(Also from the census, not changed: `depreciation` and `gaap_accrued` are in-session names with no map entry and land as `manual`; `isSystemPostedEntry` still recognises depreciation by its stamped `kind`.)*
- **C530 — THE TRANSACTIONS LIST HAD ITS OWN DEFINITION OF "NEEDS REVIEW" (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The list's pill and its *Needs Review* filter read *confidence under 70, any amount* (plus the approval flags); Review's queue and Home's *"N transactions still need a look"* read `flaggedForReview` — uncertain AND material, per entry on the entry's total (`C518`). So a $5 receipt at 60% wore the pill while Review listed nothing, and a $3,000 bill at 72% read *Booked* on the list while Home said it still needed a look — §12's forked-definition warning, on two screens a person reads in the same minute. The list reads the shared rule now (`flaggedForReview` over the ledger, keyed by entry), plus a row a person flagged.
- **C529 — THE MATCHING SCREEN LISTED A TWO-LINE BILL AS "2000 · ACCOUNTS PAYABLE $520.00" (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The faithful fixture (`C527`) rendered on the reviewer's screens: `matchableOpenItems` represents a multi-line bill by its A/P leg row, and the open-payables panel printed that row's own account. Presented through `presentLegRow` now (exported from `reports.js`; the Bills screen's `C513` rule): the first line's category, the line count, the leg's amount. `markBillPaid` re-derives the real leg row from the ledger (`C503`), so a presented row settles correctly.
- **C528 — A BILL A LIVE PAYMENT LINKS IS NOT OPEN, ON EVERY LIST AND TOTAL — WHATEVER ITS FLAG SAYS (2026-09-15). FIVE MUTATIONS, FIVE REDS.** §9 has recorded the rule since `O73`: *openness = "no LIVE clearing entry links this bill"*, and `matchableOpenItems` has read it that way all along — while every open LIST and TOTAL (`openReceivables`, `openReceivablesGL`, `openPayablesGL`, `computeAR`/`computeAP`, the aging, the `ar_tie`/`ap_tie` control totals) read `payment_status` alone. Writing a rendered test for "Money owed to you" with a collection entry whose flag write was lost (`C466`'s class) showed the split on one screen: the headline `$300` (GL) beside *"2 open invoices"* and *"$500 past due"* (flag). `settledBases(invoices)` (`gl.js`) is the live settlements' targets as one set — voided or deleted ones link nothing — and every open list and total excludes it; the paid list includes it. **It only ever REMOVES from the open set** (a linked live settlement is proof of clearing) and never adds, so nothing that counted before stops counting for any other reason; `C517`'s four ties still hold over every shape. `isSettlementEntry` moved to `gl.js` (one definition; `bankMatch` re-exports). The receivables screen's *Collected* card, its sort and each row's badge read the same open list now instead of the flag — and *Mark Paid* is withheld on a linked-settled bill (the list and the panel hand `classifyTxn` the ledger's settled set), rather than offered and refused by `markBillPaid`'s probe on click (`O124`). And `markBillPaid` asks the same in-session settled set before its DB probe — its catch read *"probe failed — fall through and post; a true dup is rare"*, which is the second-payment direction (`C245`), and the books on screen already knew.
- **C527 — THE RENDER FIXTURE'S ROWS COME OUT OF THE REAL FLATTEN NOW, AND THE FIRST RUN FOUND HOME'S ACTIVITY FEED LISTING A BILL'S OFFSET LEG (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C319`'s populated fixture hand-built its ledger rows (`C358` patched the offset leg on by hand), so every rendered screen test exercised a row shape production never makes — no `description`, no `due_date`, no `import_metadata`, and no expanded multi-line entry at all, which is the shape the whole `C503`–`C523` class lived in. The fixture is journal ENTRIES now, in the shape `fetchLedgerEntries` returns, run through `flattenJournalEntries` against the fixture's own chart; a two-line Sysco bill (`i7`) is in it. **`homeOwnerCopy` went red on the spot: Home's Activity feed listed that bill as three lines — its Food line, its Freight line, and "Sysco — Accounts Payable −$520.00"** — the offset leg, an accounting word on the owner's first screen, missed by every sweep because none had a real expanded row to draw. One line per entry now at the entry's amount, with the *· N lines* hint. *(`vendorSummary`'s "a supplier no row carries" probe used Sysco, which the fixture now carries — re-aimed at a name nothing has.)*
- **C526 — A FAILURE ROW QUOTING THE DATABASE'S OWN ERROR COULD REACH HOME'S ACTIVITY FEED (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Home admits audit rows by keyword (`paid|approv|reject|recode|void|flag|info_requested`) and then hands them to `ownerActivityText`, whose system-row exclusion named only `*_write_failed` and `*_stamp_failed`. `settled_flag_resync_failed` (`C466`) matches *flag* and its detail reads *"… couldn't be marked paid (update matched 0 rows) — …"* — a raw PostgREST sentence on the owner's first screen, the `C415` class on the one surface that rule never covered. Every `*_failed` / `*_failure` row is out of the owner feed now: the failure was toasted in the owner's words when it happened, and the audit row keeps the raw reason for the accountant. Real events with those keywords in their names still show.
- **C525 — "POST NOW" ON A RECURRING CHARGE HAS NEVER RECORDED THE RUN: THE WRITER NAMED A COLUMN THE TABLE DOES NOT HAVE (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** The `C524` method one table over — what does each writer store, and does the table have it? `recordRecurringRun` (`C360`) wrote `{ last_run, next_date }`; the column is `last_run_date` (baseline), so PostgREST refused the update on every press since it shipped, the rule never advanced, and the honest fallback `C360` wrote for a lost write — *"we couldn't record the run on the rule, so it may show as due again after a reload. Don't post it twice"* — fired every single time: a permanent failure wearing the sentence written for a rare one (`C308`'s shape). **The `C360` test pinned the writer's literal patch against itself** — it asserted `{ last_run, next_date }` was written and never asked the schema (`·3a`); re-aimed at the one patch builder, `recurringRunPatch`, whose keys `tests/recurringColumns.test.js` now parses out of the baseline DDL, with the insert's keys beside them — **and `tests/writeColumns.test.js` is now the standing census: every top-level key of every inline `insert`/`update`/`upsert`/checked-patch literal in `src/` is held to the baseline DDL plus every later `add column`, brace-balanced so a key inside `import_metadata: {…}` is not read as a column; it refuses to pass vacuously (must find the tables and the writes) and a planted `bogus_col` on a real write goes red.** **And the same diff found `project`: written by the rule's insert, never mapped back on load**, so a rule filed to a project posted to *General* after any reload. Mapped now.
- **C524 — A TAXED INVOICE SENT FROM THE APP BLOCKED SIGN-OFF AFTER A RELOAD: THE MULTI-LINE POST STAMPED NOTHING THE RPC DROPS (2026-09-15). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS. ▶ LIVE RESIDUE FOR THE OPERATOR.** Reading `persistMultiLineEntry` for what it does after the RPC — nothing — and asking what `buildArInvoiceEntry` puts in `meta`: `kind`, `invoice_number` and `tax`. `post_journal_entry` keeps six scalars of `p_meta` and discards the rest (`O95`), so a taxed invoice from Send Invoice was stored with `import_metadata: null`; `taxChargedOnInvoices` (`C241`/`C305`) reads `tax_amount` and found nothing, and `sales_tax_tie` read **$0 charged against a real $99 liability — HIGH, sign-off blocked, on correct books**: `C241`'s inverted guard, on the A/R path it never covered. **Demonstrated through the real builder and the RPC's keep-six, not asserted** — and `C517`'s shapes test could not have seen it because its fixture handed the check `import_metadata: { tax_amount: 99 }` directly, the `·3a` shape the note above it names. `multiLineMetaPatch` (pure, `entryMetaStamp.js`) is the follow-up checked update `persistJournalEntry` has carried since `C241`/`C478`: `tax_amount` under the key the control reads, the entry's `kind`, and the invoice number into `reference_number` so `C523`'s flatten reads it back; a lost stamp is audited and, for a taxed invoice, said. The test crosses the seam: builder → keep-six → stamp → flatten → the check ties. **Residue: every taxed invoice sent before this deploy carries no stamp** — `supabase/verify/C524_ar_invoice_tax_stamp.sql` counts them per company and carries the repair (commented; it stamps each entry's tax from its OWN Sales Tax Payable credit line, never a guess), the operator's to run.
- **C523 — THE UPLOAD-TIME DUPLICATE CARD ASKED ABOUT A WEEKLY TWO-LINE BILL EVERY WEEK, AND A MULTI-LINE INVOICE'S NUMBER WAS NEVER READ BACK (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The `C310` rhythm suppression asked `classifyCadence` over the vendor's ROWS — for a weekly linen bill booked as `Dr Linen 130 / Dr Delivery 15 / Cr A/P 145` that is three rows a week at three amounts, which is not a flat fee, so the question `C310` closed for single-line vendors stayed open for every multi-line one (demonstrated: over rows the rhythm reads false, over entries true). `findDuplicate` itself matched the re-drop only by accident — the A/P leg row carries the total — and the by-number match returned a LINE, so the card quoted its share. Both checks read `perEntry(invoices)` now. **And the by-number check could not have fired on a multi-line bill at all: `C478` read `reference_number` back on the flatten's simple branch only** — the expanded branch never carried `invoice_number`, so the one exact discriminator a recurring vendor's invoices have was thrown away for exactly the bills whose amounts cannot tell them apart. Carried on both branches now; the test pins that every row of a multi-line bill knows its number. *(A field-by-field diff of the flatten's two branches found the same omission on `payment_reference` and `payment_notes` — the detail panel and the Bills screen print them on a paid bill, and a multi-line one read "—"; carried now. The approval-workflow fields are simple-only and dead since `C347`; `secondary_amount` is simple-only by design, `C289`.)*
- **C522 — THE VENDOR AND CUSTOMER CARDS LISTED A BILL'S LINES AS SEPARATE TRANSACTIONS, AND READ OPEN/PAID OFF THE FLAG (2026-09-15). FIVE MUTATIONS, FIVE REDS — ONE AFTER THE CUSTOMER WIRING WAS PINNED AS STRUCTURE.** The last per-row readers on the owner's records screens: a vendor's *All transactions (N)* counted lines and listed a two-line $520 bill as two rows of $500 and $20; a customer's listed a taxed invoice at its ex-tax revenue share; each row's Open/Paid read `payment_status` — so a purchase paid at the till (`Dr Expense / Cr Cash`, no flag) read *Open* on the card whose totals `C453` had already moved off that flag; and *Payment history (N)* counted a paid bill once per line and never counted the till purchase. One row per entry now (`collapseExpandedRows`, with the *· N lines* hint the Transactions list uses), the vendor row at the entry's amount and the customer row at the receivable's (`owedAmount`, tax included, `C454`), and status from the same open lists the cards' figures read (`openBillsFor` / `openReceivables`); *Payment history* is every entry not still open. The totals were already right and are untouched. *(SSR cannot select a customer — the detail is local state — so the customer wiring is held as structure; the vendor card is rendered.)*
- **C521 — THE CHAT'S SEARCH LISTED A TWO-LINE BILL AS THREE TRANSACTIONS, ONE OF THEM "ACCOUNTS PAYABLE $6,000" (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** `search_transactions` — the tool the model quotes from — listed every flattened row: a `Dr Food 3,000 / Dr Freight 3,000 / Cr A/P 6,000` bill came back as `total_count: 3`, three rows all `kind: "entry"`, and *"the $6,000 Sysco bill"* (`min_amount: 6000`) matched only the A/P leg, so the model would have described the purchase as an Accounts Payable entry. `C486` fixed the TOTAL (signed P&L, so the three rows summed to $6,000 and not $12,000) and left the count and the list per line. One row per entry now at the entry's total, its lines beneath it with their side (so the model can tell what was bought from what is owed); a GL-code filter matches an entry carrying that code on any line and the total narrows to those lines (*"spend on Freight with Sysco"* is the Freight line, not the bill); a simple entry lists exactly as before. *(Follow-through in the same commit: the legacy no-tools prompt's ledger snapshot — `Current Ledger (N entries)` — listed and counted the same expanded rows, with `$4000`-style money; it lists per entry through `fmtSignedMoney`, and says `(+2 more lines)` on a multi-line one.)*
- **C520 — "DELETE THE $6,000 SYSCO BILL" FROM THE CHAT FOUND NOTHING, AND A FOUR-LINE BILL WAS REFUSED AS A BULK DELETE OF FOUR (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The C519 question asked of the AI's destructive-action path: `resolveActionTargets` matched flattened ROWS, so an amount the model quoted from the bill matched no line, a four-line bill counted as four items against the bulk cap of three (*"Refused a request to delete 4 items at once"* — about one transaction), the confirm card read *"Delete 3 transactions"* listing `Sysco · $2,000.00` three times, and *void by vendor* offered each line of one bill to the reverser in turn (the DB probe refused the second and third, and the reply then said *"undid 1 · couldn't undo 2"*). **And `App.jsx` carried two more copies of the matcher** — the bulk count and the executor each re-filtered `invoices` themselves, while the gate's own comment said it existed *"to keep the displayed set === the executed set"*. One resolver now, per entry at the entry's total (`perEntry`, moved into `txnPresent.js` beside `collapseExpandedRows`, re-exported from `insights.js`); an id cited off any of an entry's lines resolves to the entry; the bulk count and every executor branch read it; a recode deliberately stays per line, since each line has its own category. A source guard holds `App.jsx` to the one resolver.
- **C519 — THE UNUSUAL-ACTIVITY DETECTOR READ A BILL'S LINES AS SEPARATE CHARGES, SO A TWO-LINE BILL WAS A DUPLICATE OF ITSELF (2026-09-15). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** The C518 question asked of `runAnomalyDetection`: its population was every flattened expense row, and a bill of `Dr Food 3,000 / Dr Freight 3,000 / Cr A/P 6,000` — demonstrated through the real flatten — raised a **HIGH `duplicate_payment` against its own two lines** (which blocks sign-off), two `round_number` cards for one entry, and a `large_transaction` on the $3,000 line rather than the $6,000 the vendor charged; three lines read as *"3 charges in 48 hours"*, and a monthly two-line bill was never suggested as a recurring charge because its two same-day lines failed the cadence test (silently). Every vendor-shaped detector now reads `perEntry(rows)` — one row per entry at the entry's total, through the same `collapseExpandedRows` the Transactions list and C518 use — while `category_spike` deliberately keeps the per-line rows, since a bill with a Food line and a Freight line genuinely spent in both (pinned: the Freight surge fires, the whole bill does not). The negative case is pinned so the collapse cannot go blind: two separate two-line bills two days apart at the same total are still one duplicate, and the card names $6,000. *(Two fingerprint-stability fixtures renumbered their rows to `re_0` / `other_0` — an underscore, the flatten's expanded-row sentinel, C288's recorded collision — and dropped out of the population; real ids are uuids or floats, so the fixtures were repaired, not the sentinel.)*
- **C518 — REVIEW FLAGS WERE JUDGED PER LINE, SO A MULTI-LINE BILL WAS FLAGGED TWICE OR NOT AT ALL (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The same question asked of the reviewer's queue: `flaggedForReview` ran the materiality rules over every flattened row, so a $3,000 two-line bill at 62% raised two flags (one per $1,500 line — *"2 transactions need a look"* for one bill) and an $1,800 bill split 900/900 at the same confidence raised none (each line under the $1,000 line, the bill over it). One judgement per entry now, on the entry's total (`collapseExpandedRows`); the sign-off readiness count and Home's *"N transactions still need a look"* read the same list.
- **C517 — THE FOUR TIES, OVER EVERY SHAPE OF THE DAY, PINNED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS (2026-09-15). TEST ONLY.** `C289` swept the control totals on the shapes that existed then; the readers have since learned the taxed three-line invoice, the multi-line bill and the capitalized purchase on terms. One fixture holding all of them — with a collection and a payment behind the flags — ties on `ar_tie`, `ap_tie`, `sales_tax_tie` and `trial_balance` through the real flatten; drop the collection or the payment and the corresponding tie fires by the exact amount. Recorded as a standing check because a session that touches this many readers of one fact needs the fact re-proved at the end, not assumed from the parts.
- **C516 — THE DOCUMENTS CARD NAMED A TWO-LINE BILL AT ITS FIRST LINE'S AMOUNT (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `linkedEntryFor` returns the first row of the entry a document became, and the card printed that row's `amount` — *Sysco · $500.00* for a $520 bill — and `C346`'s search haystack indexed the same figure, so the total on the bill itself found nothing. Both read the entry's total now. *(My first test passed vacuously — it called the filter with a key it does not take, so the query was empty and every document matched; the negative case — the first line's figure must NOT find it — is what proved the fix.)*
- **C515 — THE VENDORS TAB READ A TWO-LINE BILL AS "$0 STILL OWED" AND ITS LINES AS PAID (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Rendering the vendor drill with `C498`'s fixture: *Bills you still owe $0.00 · Paid YTD $630.00* over one open $520 bill and one paid $110 bill. Two causes on one line: the vendor's rows (`txnsForVendor`) are pre-filtered to EXPENSE-account rows, so the A/P leg row that represents a multi-line bill (`C498`) never reached the open-bill test; and *Paid YTD* excluded open rows by ROW id, so an open bill's expense rows — not in the open list, whose representative is the leg — fell through as paid. Open bills are read off the vendor's full ledger rows now, openness is decided per entry (db id), and paid spend sums expense-account rows only (`C216`'s rule) so a paid multi-line bill's A/P leg is never added to its own lines. Rendered: *Paid YTD $110 · Bills you still owe $520*.
- **C514 — THE AGING REPORT'S BUCKETS COULD NOT SUM TO THE HEADLINE PRINTED ABOVE THEM (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Its headline is the GL balance (`C513`'s freezer included); its buckets were built from the flag predicate — no freezer, two rows for a two-line bill — under a comment saying *"in clean books they sum to this total"*. With the codes the buckets come from the same code-aware lists as everything else; Reports passes both roles. Without a code, the old rows, for a caller that has none.
- **C513 — A CAPITALIZED PURCHASE ON TERMS WAS IN "YOU OWE" AND ON NO BILL LIST (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** Rendering the Bills screen with `C498`'s fixture: a two-line bill was *two* bills there, each with *Mark as Paid* — the screen reads the flag list (`openPayables`, per row). Reading the predicates behind the lists found the larger thing: `openPayablesGL` — Home's *Bills to pay* card, the Vendors tab — called the code-LESS `apUnpaid` inside, which cannot see `C309`'s capitalized purchase on terms (`Dr Equipment / Cr A/P`, the live Sabine bill). So the $4,625 was counted by *You owe* on Home's health block, the `ap_tie` control total, the KPI strip and the monthly report — and listed by nothing: not the card, not the tab, not the Bills screen. `C309` recorded *"threaded to all four callers"*; those were `computeAP`'s, and the open LISTS were never among them. `openPayablesGL` uses the code-aware predicate now; the Bills screen reads it (and `paidPayablesGL`, the same rule on the paid side) when the company's A/P code resolves, falling back to the flag lists only when it cannot; a multi-line bill's representative — its A/P leg row — is presented by its first expense line's category with the line count, so the screen never prints *"2000 · Accounts Payable"* as a bill's category. **And the Bills screen's *Paid* total summed `signedPL`, which signs a non-P&L leg as a credit — a paid capitalized purchase would have read −$900 in a total of bills settled**; it sums the bills' amounts now (one live bill per row, corrections excluded by the predicate). Proved on the real flatten: the open list ties to the A/P balance AND to `computeAP`, and the screen lists the freezer.
- **C512 — AND A TYPED INVOICE NUMBER ALREADY IN USE WAS ACCEPTED (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The number is editable on the form and nothing refused a repeat, so two customers could hold the same number. It joins `C412`'s blocker list — *"Invoice number INV-0004 is already used — pick another."* — shown before the click, the buttons disabled while it stands, the handler reading the same list; a draft is not blocked against its own stored row.
- **C511 — THE NEXT INVOICE NUMBER WAS A COUNT, SO A NUMBER COULD BE SENT TO A CUSTOMER TWICE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `INV-${sentInvoices.length + 1}`: after a persist that failed (`C350`'s own case — *"in your books, but the invoice itself won't be here after a reload"*), a removed invoice, or a custom-numbered one, the next document got a number already on a customer's desk. `nextInvoiceNumber` is one past the highest `INV-` number seen.
- **C510 — "INVOICE DETAIL · VENDOR: ACME" OVER AN INVOICE THE OWNER SENT TO ACME (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The same page labelled every party *Vendor* and every entry *INVOICE DETAIL*. It reads the direction now (`classifyTxn`): a sale says *Customer* under *SALE*, a purchase *Supplier* under *PURCHASE*, a settlement *PAYMENT RECEIVED* / *PAYMENT MADE*. *(The vendor/supplier word on this screen follows the ledger row `C495` added; when the operator picks one it changes here with the rest.)*
- **C509 — THE "FULL ENTRY" PAGE SHOWED TWO ACCOUNTS AND ONE LINE'S AMOUNT FOR A THREE-LINE INVOICE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C504`'s hint says *"3 lines (see Full entry)"*; the page it names rendered the representative row's fields — *Amount $1,200 · Category Sales · Against Accounts Receivable* — and the tax line was nowhere. A door to a locked room, built the same hour. The page now headlines the entry's total and, for an expanded entry, lists every line with its account and signed amount under *THE WHOLE ENTRY · N LINES*; a simple entry is unchanged. Rendered for both.
- **C508 — HOME'S "VIEW TRANSACTION →" ON AN UNUSUAL-ACTIVITY CARD MISSED ANY MULTI-LINE ENTRY (2026-09-15).** A stored anomaly ref is the entry's db id (`C307`); an expanded entry's rows carry that on `db_entry_id` with `_n` ids of their own, and Home's `openTxn` matched `i.id` alone — so the card's door fell through to the unfiltered Transactions list (`C347`'s "where did everything go"). The bell's `txn:` door and Review's evidence resolver already index both; Home does now. *(A sweep of the other `invoice_ids`/`entity_refs` readers found none left on `i.id` alone.)*
- **C507 — UNDO PUT BACK ONE ROW OF A MULTI-LINE ENTRY (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Asked of `C503`: the list now hands the delete ONE row per entry, and `softDeleteInvoices` snapshotted exactly what it was handed — so its Undo, and its refused-delete restores, put back the representative row alone. A taxed invoice undone came back as its revenue row: the A/R and tax lines were gone from state until the next reload, and GL A/R (the trust panel's `ar_tie`, the balance sheet) read short by the invoice meanwhile. Pre-existing for any multi-line entry deleted from the list; the collapse made it every one. The whole family — every row sharing the entry's `db_entry_id` — is snapshotted at delete time, removed together, and restored together on refusal and on Undo; the settlement-flag resync (`C466`) counts the restored family. *(`C466`'s pin named the old variable and was re-aimed.)*
- **C506 — A TWO-LINE BILL WAS "2 TRANSACTIONS" ON THE VENDORS TAB AND THE BY VENDOR REPORT (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The totals were right (both sum every expense line, credits subtracting); the counts were per flattened row. `buildVendorSummary` and `computeVendorTotals` count distinct entries now, keyed on `db_entry_id` (the base id every expanded row carries).
- **C505 — THE LEDGER WAS FLATTENED AGAINST THE BUILT-IN CHART WHENEVER IT LOADED FIRST (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `flattenJournalEntries` stamps `ar_amount` — the receivable incl. tax — by finding the company's `accounts_receivable` role in the chart it is handed, and `loadAllData` handed it `CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS` as its closure saw it: the BUILT-IN chart until `useAccounts` lands, which is a race with the ledger fetch on every load and every switch. A company whose A/R is numbered anything but 1100 (§4's whole premise) then had no `ar_amount` on any taxed invoice — *Money owed to you*, the Customers screen and the chat all read ex-tax — until a reload happened to order the reads the other way; on the default numbering the two charts agree and it was invisible. `C461` made the WRITERS wait for the chart; the load now waits the same way (briefly, then proceeds with what it has — a failed chart read already blocks writes), checks the company has not changed meanwhile (`C413`), and reads the chart through a per-render ref rather than the stale closure. Demonstrated on a chart with A/R at 1150: built-in → no `ar_amount`; the company's → 1,299.
- **C504 — AND THE DETAIL PANEL HEADLINED $1,200 FOR THE $1,299 ROW JUST CLICKED (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The panel resolves its row from the raw ledger, where `C503`'s total is not stamped, so it showed one line's share. `entryTotalOf(row, rows)` sums the entry's debits across its sibling rows; the headline reads it, and *Category* says *"· 3 lines (see Full entry)"*. Pinned in source — the panel is a portal SSR cannot draw (`C246`'s recorded limit).
- **C503 — THE TRANSACTIONS LIST SHOWED A TAXED INVOICE AS THREE ROWS (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** Rendering the owner's list with the entries `C497`–`C499` were about: `flattenJournalEntries` expands a multi-line entry into one row per line (right for the P&L derivations), and the list rendered every line — an invoice from Send Invoice was *an A/R row with no amount · "Sales +$1,200 · Open · Mark Received" · "Sales Tax Payable −$99 · Paid"*, and a bill with two expense lines was three rows carrying two Mark Paid buttons. One sent invoice, three things to read, two of them nonsense, and the amount on the readable one was the ex-tax figure. `collapseExpandedRows` (pure) shows one row per entry — the revenue row for an invoice (it carries `ar_amount`), else the first P&L row — at the ENTRY's total ($1,299, $520) with a *"· 3 lines"* hint; the row keeps its own id so a click, a tick and Mark Paid reach the entry as before; the search reads the printed total. **And Mark Paid from any expanded row now settles through the entry's LEG row** (`C499`'s builder), so a bill's expense row pays the whole bill rather than flipping the flag with nothing posted. Home's drills and the Reports drill-downs stay per line — they are category-scoped by design. *(`C479`'s pin anchored on the filter's old spelling and was re-aimed at the predicate.)*
- **C502 — A TWELVE-MONTH LEASE READ "13 MONTHS LEFT" ON HOME (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The same `/ 86400000` census: the commitments card's *months left* was `ceil((end − now) / 30 days)` on a UTC-midnight parse — 365 days is 13 by that arithmetic, and a lease ending in exactly a year read a month long. `monthsLeftYMD` counts calendar months from the two strings, a partial month counting as one and a past end as none; the card reads it.
- **C501 — THE CHAT CALLED AN INVOICE "1 DAY OVERDUE" AT 7 PM ON ITS DUE DATE, FROM ANY US ZONE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED — RUN UNDER `TZ=America/Chicago`.** A census of every `/ 86400000` in `src/`: the string-vs-string differences (aging, Recon, the duplicate windows) are exact whatever the zone, `tax.js` builds local dates, and `reports.js`' `daysOverdue` anchors at local noon. One displayed integer came from a MIXED parse — `get_overdue_invoices` subtracted `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")` (UTC midnight) from a local `now`, so from Chicago the count rolled to 1 at 7 pm on the due day; the model then relayed *"1 day overdue"* about an invoice not yet late. It reads `daysOverdue` now (exported), the tool takes an injectable `now` for the test, and the test forces the zone and asserts the change took — under UTC the old expression is right by coincidence and the mutation survives (`C290`).
- **C500 — THE CHAT'S OVERDUE LIST REPORTED A TAXED INVOICE AT ITS EX-TAX AMOUNT (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `get_overdue_invoices` read `i.amount` — the revenue row's ex-tax figure — so a $1,299 invoice was quoted as $1,200 owed, the `C454` shape in the model's answer. `owedAmount` now. *(Also verified, no change: `C466`'s settlement-flag resync and the delete/undo pairing key on `db_entry_id`, which every expanded row carries, so `C498`'s representative row does not change what they find.)*
- **C499 — A TAXED INVOICE COLLECTED THROUGH A BANK MATCH WAS FLAGGED "COLLECTED" WITH NO CASH MOVEMENT (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** Following `C497`'s thread to the money: `paymentNeedsGLMovement` / `buildPaymentEntry` recognised ONE row shape — a row whose OFFSET is A/R or A/P (a simple bill or invoice). The bank matcher hands over a taxed invoice's A/R LEG row (`Dr A/R 1,299` — its PRIMARY is A/R, its offset is Revenue; it was the first row the matcher found before `C498` and is the representative after), the builder saw an offset of Revenue, returned null, and `markBillPaid` took the *"direct-to-cash, stay flag-only"* branch: the invoice read *collected*, A/R kept the full $1,299, and the deposit — held out of standalone booking because it "cleared" something — never entered the books. Cash understated and A/R overstated by every taxed invoice collected this way, since Send Invoice started booking tax (`C267`). `settlementLegOf` recognises both shapes now: the offset row (with `ar_amount` for the receivable), and the LEG row of an expanded entry — a debit to A/R or a credit to A/P carrying the whole balance. A debit to A/P (a settlement's own leg) and a credit to A/R are never bills; and on an expanded row the OFFSET branch is accepted only for the revenue row flatten marks with `ar_amount` — a tax line or one expense line of a multi-line bill carries part of the balance and is refused (the old rule accepted the tax row: `Dr Cash 99 / Cr A/R 99` for a "collection" of the tax). Proved through the real flatten and the real matcher universe: the leg row and the revenue row build the identical `Dr Cash 1,299 / Cr A/R 1,299`. **Scope correction (2026-09-15, found while closing the thread): Send Invoice's own *Mark paid* passes the base entry id, and `markBillPaid` resolves that to the FIRST expanded row — the A/R leg — so it was flag-only for every taxed invoice too, not only the bank-match path.** Fixed by the same change.
- **C498 — A MULTI-LINE BILL WAS MISSING FROM "BILLS TO PAY" AND LISTED THREE TIMES ON THE MATCHING SCREEN (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `C497`'s question asked of the A/P side, through the real flatten: a bill with two expense lines (Dr Food 500 / Dr Freight 20 / Cr A/P 520) expands to three rows. `openPayablesGL` — Home's *Bills to pay* card and the Vendors tab's *Bills you still owe* — ignores the offset on expanded rows (`touchesAccount`'s `_` rule) and found NONE of them: $120 owed on screen against $640 in the books. `matchableOpenItems` — the bank matcher's universe and the Matching screen's *Open payables* panel (`C358`) — accepted all three, so a $520 bill stood as $1,040 of open items and the engine could match a $500 ACH to the food line alone; the taxed invoice `C497` fixed had the same triple listing there (A/R row, revenue row, tax row). One row per entry now: the A/R, A/P or accrued LEG row — the one whose primary account is the receivable or payable, carrying the full amount; a debit to A/P (a payment) is never a bill. A settlement linked to the base entry still clears every row of it (`db_entry_id` carries the base id on expanded rows, so the cleared set was already right). *(Multi-line bills are rare on today's write paths — uploads book two lines — which is why the card's miss had not been seen; the matcher's triple listing applied to every taxed invoice.)*
- **C497 — A TAXED INVOICE ON TERMS WAS OWED THREE TIMES ON "MONEY OWED TO YOU" AND NOT AT ALL ON HOME (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** Found threading `C496`: Send Invoice books `Dr A/R 1,299 / Cr Revenue 1,200 / Cr Sales Tax 99` (`C267`), and flatten expands that to three rows, every one carrying A/R on a leg. `C452`'s `openReceivables` — *"an entry that carries the A/R account on a leg"* — returned all three, so *Money owed to you* listed a $1,299 invoice three times and the Customers screen's *Still owed* read **$2,697**. Live since this morning: `C452`'s fixtures were hand-made two-line rows (the `·3a` shape — the writer's own three-line output was never run). And Home's `openReceivablesGL` had the opposite defect: `touchesAccount` ignores the offset on expanded rows, so it MISSED every taxed invoice. **One row IS the receivable — the revenue row whose offset is A/R** (it is the row flatten stamps `ar_amount` on); `isReceivableRow` in `reports.js` is the one predicate now, read by `receivables.js`, Home's GL list and `computeAR`. Proved through the real flatten with the chart: one row per invoice, the total equals `glAccountBalance(A/R)`, both screens render $1,999 for two invoices. *(Three older fixtures in `controlTotals`/`aiTools` were hand-made rows with no `secondary_gl_code` — a field flatten always emits; given the field rather than the rule loosened. And the no-code case had to be re-pinned to EMPTY: my first `openReceivablesGL` fell back to the flag list without a code, which `reports.test.js` correctly refused.)*
- **C496 — THE KPI STRIP'S "WHAT CUSTOMERS OWE" WAS THE FLAG SUM OF EX-TAX REVENUE, AND THE MONTHLY REPORT ON THE REPORTS SCREEN NEVER GOT THE A/P CODE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, TWO REDS.** Reading the maths behind `C485`'s renamed labels: `computeKPIs` built *what customers owe* as Σ `amount` over the `payment_status` list — the `C452` flag-openness and the `C454` ex-tax amount, on the strip that sits beside an aging report reading the GL — and *what's due* the same way on the A/P side. It takes `arCode`/`apCode` now and reads `glAccountBalance` as of the strip's date (§12's one source); without codes the fallback at least counts the receivable INCLUDING its tax (`owedAmount`). **And threading the codes found a caller `C309` missed: `MonthlyReportsPanel` — the monthly report a person opens on Reports — never passed `apCode` to `buildMonthlyReport`, so its *You owe* was the flag sum while Home's was the GL balance; the `C309` pin checked `App.jsx`'s call and not the panel's.** Both callers pass both codes now, and the pin names the panel.
- **C495 — "DUE TODAY" ON A RECURRING CHARGE THREE WEEKS OVERDUE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Seen in `C492`'s file: the badge was the literal *Due today* for any rule whose `next_date <= today`. `dueLabel` reads the gap (*Due yesterday · Due 10 days ago · Due 3 weeks ago · Due 3 months ago*), rendered for an overdue rule. *(Noted while here, not changed: a recurring rule is an EXPECTATION — the Home card's own words are "so it's always expected" — and *Post* on it is a manual step on a Settings screen; nothing on Home says a rule is due, and a rule posted by hand beside a bank feed that also books the charge is the `O123` double-post shape. Whether rules should post at all when a bank feed exists is a design question, recorded rather than decided.)* Also recorded: nav says **Vendors**, the same screen and Settings say **suppliers** — one vocabulary is `C320`'s rule and the word is the operator's call; ledger row added.
- **C494 — `C489`–`C491` MADE THE TOTALS SIGNED AND LEFT THEM ON THE FORMATTER THAT DROPS THE SIGN (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, TWO REDS.** Probing the money formatters on negatives: `fmtMoney(-5)` is `$5.00`, by design — it is the magnitude formatter for tables that colour by direction, and `C146` says so. The five views whose totals the last three commits made leg-signed all alias `fmt = fmtMoney`, and their colours are fixed per section (expenses always red) rather than by sign. So a category whose only movement this period is a correction — the bill it corrects booked in August, the correction in September — nets −$500 and printed **$500.00** in the same red as a charge: the figure the fix had just made right, with its sign hidden at the last step. A supplier whose refund exceeded the year's purchases read as money paid; a customer's net credit read as money billed. Those five views alias `fmt` to `fmtSignedMoney` now (as Recon, Review and Contracts already did); per-row `amount`s are magnitudes by construction, so nothing else on those screens changes. Rendered: the correction-only month prints `-$500.00`. *(And `noUndefinedRefs` caught my import cleanup dropping a `fmtMoney` that was passed by NAME, not called — a reference the render test's branch never reached. Fourth catch by that guard this week.)*
- **C493 — THE CHAT'S FALLBACK SNAPSHOT CALLED ZERO OR OVERDRAWN CASH "NOT SET BY THE OWNER YET" (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Reading what the model is told after `C486`–`C488` fixed what the tools return: the no-tools fallback bakes a financial snapshot into the prompt, and its cash line read *"not set by the owner yet"* for any figure ≤ 0, with runway *"unknown — cash balance not set"*. Cash on hand has been GL-derived since §12's single-source rule, so an overdrawn account and a genuinely empty one both read as a missing setting — a world-claim the model relays as an instruction (*"set your cash balance first"*) about a field that is no longer how cash is known. The line states the figure whatever its sign, says *overdrawn* when negative, and runway reads *"none — there is no cash on hand to spend down"*. The category totals and receivables in the same snapshot were already signed and canonical.
- **C492 — A RECURRING CHARGE DUE ON THE 31st SKIPPED FEBRUARY (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** A census of every `getMonth()`/`setMonth()` after `C440`: all the reads are on locally-built Dates, and the one raw `setMonth(+1)` was the Recurring screen's *Post* — which OVERFLOWS: Jan 31 → "Feb 31" → **Mar 3**. A month-end rent was never offered for February, and the rule then sat on the 3rd; a quarterly rule from Nov 30 landed on Mar 2. `format.js` has carried the clamped helper since CR-4 and every schedule builder uses it; this screen predated it. **And the obvious fix — the clamp — has its own drift, recorded by `C290`:** advancing cumulatively from a clamped date parks Jan 31 → Feb 28 → Mar 28 → … on the 28th forever, and the rule stores no anchor day. `nextRecurringDate` (pure) takes the conventional answer: a date that IS its month's last day advances to the next month's last day (Jan 31 → Feb 28 → Mar 31 → Apr 30, leap Februaries honoured); any other day clamps; an unknown frequency yields null and the date is left alone rather than guessed. *(Residue, stated: a rule dated the 29th or 30th reaches Feb 28 — a last day — and follows month-ends from there; an anchor column would remove it.)* The overflow is demonstrated in the test rather than asserted, and no `setMonth`/`setFullYear` may remain in a component.
- **C491 — THE UNSIGNED-SUM CLASS, SWEPT THROUGH EVERY SCREEN (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C489` (deductions) and `C490` (the P&L's lines) were two instances; the question was how many more sum a ledger row's raw `amount` where a person reads the figure. A census of every `.amount` sum in `src/components/views`: Home's revenue and expense drills (six sums — the headline subtitles, the by-category and by-vendor breakdowns), a customer's *billed this year*, a vendor's *paid this year*, the Bills screen's paid total, the By Vendor drill, the cash-basis headline, and the Balance Sheet's own retained-earnings roll (`bsRevenue`/`bsExpenses`, which feed `ytdNet` and so the equity section). Every one counted a correction as a second purchase. `signedPL(row)` is exported from `reports.js` — an expense debit or a revenue credit counts positive, the opposite leg subtracts — and every site reads it. `tests/signedPL.test.js` is the standing census: a raw `.amount` sum in a view fails unless it is over an open-item list (one live leg per row), invoice line items, the reconcile's bank lines, or the deductions BREAKDOWN (per-category figures `C489` already signed). *(A first version of the guard flagged those four; the excusal names each with its reason, per `C249`.)*
- **C490 — THE INCOME STATEMENT'S LINES DID NOT ADD UP TO ITS OWN HEADLINE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The headline totals flow through the canonical layer (leg-signed, so a correction nets); the per-category rows beneath them — and the By Project rows — summed `amount` unsigned. A $500 bill and its $500 correction read as *$1,000 of Food Cost* on a report whose total said $0; a reversed sale doubled its revenue line. The `C489` class on the report itself: the rows are signed by the leg now (an expense debit and a revenue credit count positive; the opposite leg subtracts), rendered through the real flatten with a correction pair on each side.
- **C489 — THE DEDUCTION TRACKER COUNTED A CORRECTED BILL TWICE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `deductionBreakdown` summed `amount` unsigned per deduction account — a correction is a CREDIT to the same expense account, so a corrected $2,400 rent bill read as $4,800 of rent deductions on the Taxes screen and in the chat's deductions tool, and the estimated-tax figure fell with it. `C216` recorded the rule for the Vendors tab (*spend is a signed expense-account movement*); the tax page never got it. Signed by the leg now.
- **C488 — THE BY VENDOR REPORT AND THE CHAT'S TOP-VENDORS TOOL STILL SPLIT ONE SUPPLIER INTO TWO (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `computeVendorTotals` grouped on the display name; `C210` moved the Vendors tab onto the vendor key and `C317` onto the alias index, and this function — read by the By Vendor report and by `get_vendor_totals` — was never moved with them. *"Hill Country Milling Co."* and *"Hill Country Milling"* were two rows on the report and two vendors in the chat's answer. Grouped by `vendor_key` through a confirmed alias now, labelled by the most recent spelling (the `C210` rule); the report passes `aliasIndex` and `runAIBrain` carries it into the tool ctx. Without an alias two keys stay apart — no string surgery (`C202`).
- **C487 — THE CHAT'S OVERDUE LIST DECIDED "OPEN" FROM THE PAID FLAG ALONE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `get_overdue_invoices` had its own `unpaid` — flag only — so a bill canceled by a correction (flag still unpaid) was reported overdue, and it could disagree with `get_financial_summary` two tools over, which reads `computeAP`. It reads the same predicate now (`openPayables`/`openReceivables`, code-free on purpose — this ctx may carry no A/P code, and `C309`'s rule is never to guess one); the local predicate is deleted rather than left. *(First attempt used the code-keyed `openPayablesGL`, which reports NOTHING open without a code — two existing tests with `getAccountByRole: () => null` went red and were right to.)*
- **C486 — "HOW MUCH HAVE I SPENT WITH SYSCO?" ANSWERED $1,500 FOR A $500 BILL (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The chat's `search_transactions` tool summed `amount` over every row matching the vendor: the bill, the payment that settled it (a row carrying the vendor's name and an A/P primary), and a correction of it — and told the model `total_amount_display` was *"the exact string to quote"*. `C216` fixed the same arithmetic on the Vendors tab a fortnight ago (*"spend is an expense-account movement"*); the tool the model quotes from never got it. The total is the signed P&L movement now — a settlement contributes nothing, a correction subtracts, a revenue row counts its credit — every listed row carries a `kind` (`entry` / `payment` / `correction` / `opening_balance`) so the model can say which is which, and a `note_payments` tells it why the listed rows do not sum to the total.
- **C485 — THE SUPPLIER-RULES SCREEN'S ONLY EXAMPLE WAS A RULE THE ASSISTANT WOULD REFUSE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The empty state says *tell the assistant things like "Always tag FedEx invoices to Shipping & Freight"* — no chart has that category (`C260` refuses an account in neither chart), so the one example on the screen led to a refusal. It names *Shipping & Fulfillment* now, and a test holds the example's category to the default chart. *(And the error screen's *"head back to your dashboard"* says Home, the word the sidebar uses.)*
- **C484 — THE KPI REPORT SAID "CURRENT RATIO · GROSS MARGIN · OPERATING EXPENSE RATIO · BURN MULTIPLE · DAYS SALES OUTSTANDING" ON THE OWNER'S REPORTS SCREEN (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Its labels and explanations live in `reports.js`, so `C315`'s guard — which scans client VIEW source — never read them, and the regex bar itself only caught *"receivables"*. Renamed in the owner's words with the metrics unchanged: *Can you cover what's due? · What you keep after direct costs · Running costs as a share of sales · Cash spent to grow sales · How long customers take to pay*; the explanations drop *COGS*, *receivables*, *burn* and *revenue*; the empty states read *"No sales yet"* rather than *N/A*. Keys are unchanged (the monthly report and its tests read them). `tests/kpiOwnerWords.test.js` holds every KPI string to the bar over a populated and an empty month.
- **C483 — "-$0.00" (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Probing the money formatter at its edges: `fmtSignedMoney(-1e-13)` printed *-$0.00* and `fmtApprox(-0.4)` printed *-$0*. A net income that is exactly zero in cents commonly sums to a sub-cent negative in floating point, so *"Profit this year: -$0.00"* was reachable on Home and Reports — a minus on a zero reads as a defect. The sign follows the rounded value now; a real negative keeps it, pinned in both directions.
- **C482 — THE SETTINGS ROW "BANK ACCOUNTS & STARTING BALANCES" OPENED A SCREEN WITH NO BANK-ACCOUNT FORM (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, TWO REDS.** Found by rendering Home for a brand-new company and reading the checklist: *"Add your bank account — Settings → Bank Accounts"*, a row that does not exist. Tracing where the button goes: the bank-account form lives on the **Company** settings page (`#bank-accounts-section`); the row `C378` named *"Bank accounts & starting balances"* opens `OpeningBalancesView`, which shows bank-linked cash read-only and has nowhere to add an account. A person adding their bank clicked the obvious row and found a grid — `C321`'s locked room, with the key on a different door. The row and its heading read *"Starting balances"* now (what the screen is), the onboarding hint names the page its button opens (*Settings → Company → Bank accounts*), and the accountant step's redundant *"(optional) · Optional"* says what an accountant does instead. A test holds the row name to the screen's contents.
- **C481 — THE CASH-BASIS P&L SHOWED A CANCELED EXPENSE WITH NOTHING CANCELLING IT, AND ITS BANK-LINE TEST WAS DEAD AFTER A RELOAD (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The cash-basis filter kept a row when `source === "bank_feed"` or the flag read paid/collected. Two things wrong with that: stored bank lines read `bank_import` — the RPC normalises the in-session name — so the source test matched nothing after a reload (the lines survived only because they also carry the paid flag); and a correction (`C470`'s shape) carries no flag while the paid bill it cancels does, so the filter kept the original and dropped the correction — the canceled $500 stood on the cash-basis report. A correction and its target are kept together now (they net to zero in every case), and the source test reads the stored name. The Home cash drill's count had the same dead test.
- **C480 — TEN MORE RAW DATABASE SENTENCES ON TOASTS, UNDER `error.message` INSTEAD OF `r.error` — INCLUDING THE BOOKING PATH'S OWN (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C415`'s census keyed on the checked-write helpers' `${r.error}` tails; a bare `error.message` / `rpcErr.message` / `e.message` off a Supabase call or a `catch` walked past it. Among them: ***"Couldn't save the entry: <postgres sentence>"*** on `persistJournalEntry` and `persistMultiLineEntry` — every booking that the RPC refuses — the opening-balance post, the reversal post, adding or removing a category (*"Account code already exists."*), both Team invite actions, the QuickBooks undo, and two PARSER errors (*"Couldn't parse that file: Unexpected token…"*) that no person can act on and now simply say what to do instead (*"try exporting it as a CSV"*). All routed through `plainWriteError` — the `078` trigger's sentence still passes through untouched — and the guard now reads any `.message` of an error object inside a toast (a planner's own `plan.message` is not an error and is excluded). *(And the chat reply for a recurring rule printed `$4000` — `fmtMoney` now.)*
- **C479 — THE TRANSACTIONS SEARCH DID NOT MATCH THE AMOUNT AS PRINTED (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Its haystack held `String(824.6)`, so typing *824.60* — the figure on the row — found nothing; the printed form is in the haystack now (`C346`'s rule, one list over), and so is the invoice number `C478` keeps. Placeholder says what it searches.
- **C478 — THE INVOICE NUMBER WAS DROPPED AT THE RPC TOO, SO THE DUPLICATE-BY-NUMBER CHECK ONLY EVER SAW THE CURRENT SITTING (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `C477`'s question asked of the next field on the invoice object: `invoice_number` is extracted from every document and typed on the manual form, and both duplicate checks (the form's and the upload path's) compare it against `ex.invoice_number` on the ledger rows — which the flatten never emitted, because nothing ever wrote `journal_entries.reference_number`, a column the baseline has carried all along. So the one EXACT discriminator a flat-fee vendor's invoices have (`O117`/`O127` recorded it as the thing the payment lacks and the invoice has) was thrown away, and a re-dropped invoice after a reload was caught only by the fuzzy vendor/amount/date window — which `C310`'s rhythm suppression correctly waives for a recurring vendor. Written into `reference_number` in the same post-RPC stamp as the metadata (a lost number is audited, not toasted — it costs one duplicate check, not a figure); the flatten reads it back. *(Three `C241` pins on the stamp's exact shape were re-aimed at the property — metadata is still only written when there is something to say.)*
- **C477 — A PROJECT CHOSEN ON A BOOKING NEVER REACHED THE DATABASE (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `O95`'s class on a column that exists: `post_journal_entry` inserts `(account_id, debit, credit, memo)` per line and no `project`, and `persistJournalEntry` never sent one — so the manual form's project picker, a supplier rule's project (*"Applied your vendor rule … (Project: X)"*) and a recurring charge's project rode the in-session row onto the *By Project* report and came back as *General* on the next reload. The only writer of `journal_entry_lines.project` was the AI's retag action, which is why the column and the `079` guard (a project retag in a signed month is allowed) were both real and both idle for ordinary bookings. `stampLineProject` is the one writer now (read back to verify the value, `C274`'s stronger check), called by the retag and by `persistJournalEntry` after the post for any project other than *General*; a lost stamp is audited with the project named. *(Multi-line entries — payroll, contracts, depreciation — carry no project and are untouched.)*
- **C476 — "DEFAULT CASH ACCOUNT" AND "CURRENCY" ON SETTINGS WERE SAVED AND READ BY NOTHING (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The same census as `C473`/`C474`, on the company row: `default_cash_account`, `default_ap_account`, `default_ar_account` and `currency` are written by the Settings save, mapped back on load, and consulted by no line of `src/` — cash resolves by role and through the bank account a statement is matched to (§4), and every figure prints in US dollars whatever the picker said. Choosing *EUR* changed a column. Both controls are removed rather than wired: a currency option on a product built around 1099s and Schedule C is a promise it does not keep, and a default-cash picker beside bank accounts that each carry their own account is a second answer to a settled question. The round-trip shapes keep the columns (a schema contract, pinned elsewhere); the guard asserts no reader exists in `src/`, so the removal cannot be hiding a live setting.
- **C475 — THE INVOICE HAD NO PLACE FOR THE CUSTOMER'S ADDRESS (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The Customers form saves a mailing address (extracted from documents, or typed); the invoice a person sends printed *Bill To: Acme* and nothing under it. The draft carries `customer_address` now — prefilled from the saved customer when picked (`C473`'s path), editable, printed under *Bill To* in the email/print template and the preview. *(The `ar_invoices` row does not store it; a reloaded invoice re-sent from the app takes it from the customer again.)*
- **C474 — "MIN EXPECTED ($)" / "MAX EXPECTED ($)" HAD NO READER EITHER (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** Same census, next field: saved, loaded, displayed, consulted by nothing. A band a person declares for a supplier is precisely the fact a detector should prefer over a statistic, so it has one now — `outside_expected_range` (MEDIUM: a judgment, never a block; `O122` category 3) fires once per charge outside the band, quoting the band and the charge (*"You told us Sysco usually charges $400.00–$900.00; this one on 2026-09-10 is $2,400.00"*), reads revenue rows for a customer's band, and is silent for a supplier with no band. The runner takes `contacts`; the type is in the card-rate taxonomy (the `C253` guard would have refused it otherwise); and the labels say what the number means — *"Usually charges at least ($)"*, *"Usually pays at least ($)"* — rather than *Min Expected*.
- **C473 — "PAYMENT TERMS" ON THE VENDORS AND CUSTOMERS FORMS HAD NO READER (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The field was typed, saved (`C361` made sure of that), displayed on the contact card — and consulted by nothing: `buildUploadedInvoice` derived a bill's due date from the DOCUMENT's printed terms alone, so a supplier whose invoices never print terms got no due date on any bill, *Bills to pay* could never say one was overdue, and aging filed them as current forever; and Send Invoice opened on *Net 30* and a blank email for a customer whose card said *Net 15* and carried an address. §9's *name the reader* on a form field: it has two now. The builder takes the matched contact (through `findContactForName`, the `C366` rule) and uses its terms where the document states none — the document wins when it says; the invoice draft fills terms and email when a saved customer is picked, mapping stored text onto the select's five options by day count and leaving a term the select cannot express (Net 45) alone rather than showing a blank.
- **C472 — "DISABLE" ON A CATEGORY CHANGED NOTHING BUT THE ROW'S OPACITY (2026-09-15). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** The Categories screen has had a Disable control (`active: false`) and the business-type template hides irrelevant accounts the same way (`C223`, `coa-template:hide`). **Nothing read the flag**: the recode picker, the Review override, Bank Import's and Reconcile's category selects, the recurring form, the bank-account GL picker, the QuickBooks mapping and the starting-balances grid all listed a disabled account exactly as before — and so did **all seven AI chart slots**, so the model went on booking to a category the person had turned off. `O123` in a checkbox: the control looked obeyed. `BOOKABLE_ACCOUNTS` (`chart.js`, `active !== false`) is now what every chooser and every AI slot reads; the full chart stays the resolver for existing rows (an entry on a disabled account still has a name) and for the balance sheet (a disabled account with a balance is still on it). **And the control was offered on Cash and Accounts Payable**, where obeying it would have been worse than ignoring it — every write path resolves those by role and would have kept using them while the pickers lost them. `disableAccountBlocker` refuses a structural role and names the supplier rule, recurring charge or bank account that points at the account; `persistAccountEdit` asks it before the write. *(The guard walks every `<option>` drawn from the chart in `src/components`; three balance-sheet readers legitimately read the full chart and draw no options.)*
- **C471 — A CORRECTION MADE WITHIN A WEEK RAISED A "TWO CHARGES" CARD ON ITSELF (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The anomaly runner's population was *live expense rows with a positive amount* — which a correction is (`C470`'s shape). So a $6,200 purchase corrected two days later produced a `duplicate_payment` card (HIGH — blocks sign-off) on the bill and its own cancellation, a `large_transaction` card on the correction, and both halves in every vendor and category baseline: three extra cards on the mutation, none of them about anything a person did wrong. `isCancelledOrCancelling` is applied to the one population the runner reads; the negative case (the uncorrected purchase still raises) is pinned so the exclusion cannot go blind.
- **C470 — A CORRECTION READ AS A SECOND PURCHASE, AND THE REMOVED ORIGINAL HAD THREE NAMES (2026-09-15). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** The `C468` probe showed the shape: a correction flattens with an expense primary, no paid flag, amount +500 — so the Transactions list rendered it *"−$500 · Paid"*, dated today, one row under the original it cancels (struck through). Two Sysco purchases, one of them marked removed. `classifyTxn` now carries `correction`; money direction flips for one (a credit to expense is money back, so it reads *+$500*), and `txnStatus` says *Correction* rather than *Paid*. The shared badge (`txnStatusBadge`, on the panel and the Reports drill-down) said *Booked* for the correction and *Paid · ACH* for a removed original beside its own *Removed* marker; it reads *Correction* / *Removed* now. And the removed original was *"↩ Reversed"* on the list, *"Removed · DATE"* on the panel, *"Already corrected"* on its disabled control — one word now, the panel's (`O130`: "void" and "reversal" are bookkeeper words).
- **C469 — "CHANGE CATEGORY" WAS OFFERED ON A CORRECTION AND ON THE ENTRY IT CANCELS (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The `C468` follow-through: a correction mirrors its target line for line, so recoding either one alone leaves the cancellation on the old account — the old category is no longer canceled and the new one is charged out of nothing. The panel withholds the control with a sentence that says what to do instead (record the purchase again with the right category), `persistRecode` refuses both halves ahead of the signed-month check, and `planRecodeSweep` (`C339`) offers neither half — nor a settlement, which it had also been listing as a sibling on the same account.
- **C468 — A BILL CORRECTED IN A SIGNED MONTH WAS TWO OPEN BILLS (2026-09-15). EIGHT MUTATIONS, EIGHT REDS.** Found by asking what *Mark as Paid* does on a row marked *Removed · DATE*: nothing stopped it. The original of a dated correction stays live (§12 #14) with its A/P leg, and the correction mirrors every line — so it flattens as a bill-shaped row of its own (expense primary, A/P offset, no paid flag, amount **+500**, demonstrated through the real flatten). Every openness reader derived "open" from *A/P leg + not flagged paid* and read BOTH: *Bills to pay* listed the canceled bill and its correction as two $500 bills against a balance of $0; `matchableOpenItems` offered both to the bank matcher; the panel offered *Mark as Paid* on the canceled bill and `markBillPaid` would have posted cash out for a purchase the books no longer carry; and `ap_tie` failed by twice the corrected amount. One predicate in `gl.js` (`isCancelledOrCancelling`: the flatten stamps `reversed_by` on an original from the live reversal it sees, and a reversal is known by `import_metadata.reverses`), read by `apUnpaid`, `arUnpaid`, `openReceivables`, `matchableOpenItems` and `classifyTxn.settleAction`; `markBillPaid` refuses on GL truth (`alreadyReversed`) before the optimistic flag write. The GL sums are untouched — both entries still count, which is what makes them net to zero. *(The spelling guard fired on *"User cancelled"* in an audit sentence — the Activity feed renders audit detail, so it is user-facing; Americanized.)*
- **C467 — DELETE WAS OFFERED ON THE OPENING-BALANCE ENTRY (2026-09-15). FIVE MUTATIONS, FIVE REDS.** The transactions list carries the opening entry like any other row, and the panel's Delete went through `softDeleteJournalEntry`, which took the entry out and nothing else — the `opening_balances` rows kept `posted: true`, so the starting-balances grid stayed full, the cutoff stayed locked (*"opening balances posted"*), onboarding stayed ticked, `postOpeningBalances` refused a second (*"already recorded"*), and cash on hand read zero. The AI's `delete_invoice`/`void_invoice` and the Books bulk select reached the same door. `planEntryRemoval` returns a third mode, `KEEP`, with the sentence (*"Starting balances aren't removed here — change them on the starting-balances screen"*); `planBulkRemoval` lists them as `kept` and says so in `blocked`; `softDeleteJournalEntry` and `reverseJournalEntry` refuse the source outright (the AI paths reach those directly); the panel shows the sentence in the control's place (`O124`: not offered, not refused on click). The supersede path in `postOpeningBalances` writes its own update and is untouched.
- **C466 — REMOVING A PAYMENT LEFT ITS BILL FLAGGED PAID (2026-09-15). NINE MUTATIONS, NINE REDS — ONE AFTER ITS PIN WAS ANCHORED ON THE LINE START.** `markBillPaid` posts the payment and stamps `payment_status: "paid"` on the bill; deleting that payment (`softDeleteJournalEntry`) or correcting it in a signed month (`reverseJournalEntry`) took the GL movement out and left the flag. §9 records the flag as the one thing that still drives the paid/unpaid LISTS — so *Bills to pay* hid a bill the A/P balance still carried, *Money owed to you* hid a collected-then-uncollected invoice, and `ap_tie` failed by exactly the removed amount. The delete path already reversed the OTHER direction (a deleted bill takes its payments with it); this is its mirror. `src/lib/settlementFlag.js`: after any change to the set of live settlements linking a bill, the flag is what the live ones say — paid/collected if one remains, unpaid if none — with the just-removed (or, on an undo, the reversal) excluded by id, because state and refs lag a write by a render. Wired at four points: delete, delete-undo, reversal, reversal-undo. **And the reversal's Undo fell through to *"Restored ✓"* after its own error toast** — the `C238` shape, in the function `C238` fixed; it returns now. *(The ninth comment-guard escape: commenting the call out satisfied a pin matching the call's text; anchored on `\n\s*await` so only a live statement passes.)*
- **C465 — "CHANGE CATEGORY" WAS OFFERED ON A PAYMENT ROW (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** The detail panel showed the recode control on every row; a settlement's lines are A/P-or-A/R and cash, so recoding one moved the A/P leg to an expense, broke the link to the bill it settles (`payment_for`), and `learnFromCorrection` then taught the supplier the new account. An opening-balance entry has the same shape. The panel now renders a sentence in the control's place (*"A payment has no category of its own — change the category on the bill or invoice it settles."*), and `persistRecode` refuses both before any write, ahead of the signed-month check — the bulk sweep (`C339`) and the AI recode action reach it too. *(Pinned in source: the panel is a portal, which SSR cannot render — `C246`'s recorded limit.)*
- **C464 — RELOADING ON REPORTS DEFAULTED THE PERIOD FROM THE RESET FISCAL YEAR (2026-09-15).** The mount-only effect that sets the report window to the current fiscal year read `companySettings.fiscalYearEnd` and `cutoffDate` before the company row arrived — `"12-31"` and null — and never re-ran; a company with a June year end opened Reports on the calendar year. Runs once `companyDataLoaded` flips (`C427`'s shape). Found by a probe for mount-only effects reading state that changes; the other hit was the root's session effect, which is correct.

- **★ C463 — `ownerTrust` READ `hasAttester` AND `signoffs` WITH NEITHER IN ITS DEPS, AND A GUARD NOW HOLDS EVERY MEMO TO ITS DEPS (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** No linter runs here, so `exhaustive-deps` never did; `C347` found `vendorSummary` missing `aliasIndex` by hand. The trust panel was right only because the effect that sets `hasAttester` batches with `intakeRows`, a dep that is listed — a memo correct by virtue of React's batching is the `C261` shape. `tests/memoDeps.test.js` checks every `useMemo` in `App.jsx` against the state names its body reads; a stale memo on an accounting screen is a silently wrong number.

- **C462 — "TOTAL CASH ACROSS ACCOUNTS" ON SETTINGS WAS Σ `bank_accounts.current_balance` — THE ONE FIGURE §12 SAYS MUST NEVER BE SHOWN AS CASH (2026-09-15).** `C331` removed `cashFromBanks` for exactly this; the Settings screen had its own inline sum under a label that contradicts Home's GL-derived *Cash on hand*. Relabelled as what it is — the statement balances the person entered, added up — with a line saying Home's figure comes from the books.

- **★ C461 — A BOOKING IN THE FIRST SECOND AFTER A SWITCH RESOLVED ROLES AGAINST AN EMPTY CHART (2026-09-15).** `C416` cleared the previous company's chart on switch and refused writes after a FAILED read; between the switch and the read landing the chart is `[]`, `accountsLoadOk` is still true, and a drop (or the drain resuming) reaching the writers then would resolve every role through the built-in fallback and materialise it — the `O108` absorber, reachable by timing. `useAccounts` records which company its chart is for; both writers `await waitForChart()` (up to 8 s, polling a per-render ref) before any business rule, and refuse if it never arrives. *(A company whose read genuinely returns an empty chart still reaches the fallback — that is the absorber decision, separate.)*

- **C460 — RECONCILE'S ACCOUNT DEFAULTED TO THE PLACEHOLDER TOO, AND NEVER ADOPTED THE REAL ONE (2026-09-15).** `ReconView` seeded its account from `bankAccounts[0]` at mount — the placeholder before the accounts arrive — and, being `useState`, never moved when they did; a session opened then could not save (`C194`'s gate reported it, after the work). Seeds from the first stored (uuid) account now and adopts it on arrival if nothing was chosen.

- **C459 — AND THE REVIEWER'S BANK IMPORT HAD THE SAME DOOR: ITS PICKER DEFAULTS TO `bankAccounts[0]`, THE PLACEHOLDER (2026-09-15).** `handleBankFile` now refuses an account whose id is not a stored uuid before any parsing, with a sentence that says to wait and try again; nothing is changed. *(The placeholder itself — seeded by `resetCompanyState` so onboarding has an account to name — is the root, and stays: every consumer that could write against it now checks.)*

- **★ C458 — A STATEMENT DROPPED BEFORE THE BANK ACCOUNTS LOADED AUTO-BOUND TO THE PLACEHOLDER ACCOUNT (2026-09-15).** `resetCompanyState` seeds `{ id: "default", name: "Primary Checking" }` until the company's accounts arrive, and a failed `bank_accounts` read leaves it there; `autoBindAccount` accepted any truthy `id`, so a single-account company's statement dropped in that window bound to an account that does not exist — the lines booked (the role resolved cash correctly), the `bank_statements` insert died on a non-uuid, and no reconciliation could ever follow (`C185`'s *"rejected insert took statement + line persistence down behind a warn"*). Only a uuid-shaped id binds now; the placeholder and a failed read route the file to the accountant's stash as a 0-or-2+ account company does.

- **C457 — A BOOKING AGAINST A COMPANY WHOSE ROW DID NOT LOAD RAN WITH NO CUTOFF GUARD (2026-09-15).** The company row carries the cutoff (Day One) and the fiscal year; a failed `companies` read leaves `cutoffDate` null, which the guards read as "no enforcement" (the legacy case). Both write paths refuse while `loadFailures.companies` is set, beside `C416`'s chart gate, with their own sentence.

- **C456 — A FAILED `companies` READ LEFT THE SETTINGS FORM ON THE RESET DEFAULTS WITH SAVE ENABLED (2026-09-15).** `C427` gated the draft on `companyDataLoaded` — which flips true whether or not the company row itself loaded (`allSettled`), so on that one failure the form seeded blanks and a save would have written them. The seed and the save both gate on `loadFailures.companies` now; the screen carries the notice and the Save button is disabled.

- **★ C455 — "YOUR BOOKS HAVEN'T BEEN MATCHED TO YOUR BANK" OVER A FAILED RECONCILIATIONS READ (2026-09-15).** The last `O98` input: `bankMatchStatus` over `[]` from a failed read is the same as a company never matched, so Home's NOW item, the trust line and the bell all told the owner to drop a statement. `checked: false` returns not-overdue with `unchecked: true`; the trust line says it could not check and all-clear is unreachable; Home's memo reads `loadFailures.reconciliations`, the bell a ref of the same.

- **C454 — THE CUSTOMER'S "STILL OWED" EXCLUDED THE SALES TAX ON THE INVOICE (2026-09-15).** A taxed A/R invoice flattens to a revenue row whose `amount` is the ex-tax revenue, with the full receivable carried as `ar_amount` (`owedAmount`, the rule the aging report and "Money owed to you" already use). The Customers screen summed `amount`, so a $1,299 invoice read as $1,200 owed. `owedAmount` now; the `C438` class one screen over.

- **★★ C453 — THE VENDORS SCREEN CALLED A CARD PURCHASE A BILL YOU STILL OWE, AND COUNTED IT AS PAID NOWHERE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C452` on the supplier side: *"Bills you still owe"* was every expense row without `payment_status = paid`, so a purchase paid at the till (`Dr Expense / Cr Cash`, no status) read as owed; *"Paid YTD"* counted ONLY rows stamped paid, so the same purchase counted nowhere — a supplier paid $300 by card showed *Paid $0 · Owed $300*. Open = the A/P-leg rule the Bills screen uses (`openPayablesGL`, `C309`); paid this year = everything spent this year that is not still open. Rendered both figures with a card purchase and a bill side by side.

- **★★ C452 — "STILL OWED TO YOU" ON THE CUSTOMERS SCREEN COUNTED MONEY ALREADY IN THE BANK (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The customer drill summed every revenue row whose `payment_status` was not *collected* — and a direct deposit (`Dr Cash / Cr Revenue`, a card sale at the register) carries no status, so a customer who had PAID showed their whole year's sales as owed. **§9's flag-derived openness by name — the `O83` root cause — on the owner's Customers screen; `ArView` had the right rule (an entry carrying the A/R leg, not a settlement, not collected) since `O83` Feb and the two screens never shared it.** `src/lib/receivables.js` is the one definition now (`receivableEntries` / `openReceivables`), read by both. *(Why the `ar_tie` control total never failed on the same rows: `computeAR` is flag-derived too, but every cash-settled writer stamps `payment_status: 'paid'` (§9's cash-settled doctrine, `C189`), so bank deposits carry the flag and drop out. Correct by virtue of the writers — the `C261` shape — which is why the screens should not depend on it and now do not.)*

- **★ C451 — A SENT INVOICE WITH NO TYPED DUE DATE WAS STORED WITH NONE, SO IT COULD NEVER BE OVERDUE (2026-09-15).** `C450` fixed what the document SHOWS; the stored `ar_invoices` row and the A/R entry still carried `due_date: null` for the default *Net 30* draft, so *"Money owed to you"* could never call it overdue and the aging buckets had nothing to age. The sent invoice now carries the typed-or-derived due date. **And the re-send sync wrote `amount: subtotal` onto the in-session ledger row** — the `C438` class one line over; it writes the taxed total.

- **★ C450 — THE INVOICE READ "DUE DATE: ON RECEIPT · TERMS: NET 30" BY DEFAULT — TWO CLAIMS THAT CONTRADICT EACH OTHER, ON THE DOCUMENT A CUSTOMER PAYS FROM (2026-09-15).** The draft defaults to terms *Net 30* and an empty due date; the print fell back to *On Receipt* for the date and to *Net 30* for the terms, and the email said *"due on receipt"* beside an invoice saying Net 30. `invoiceDueLabel(draft)`: a typed due date wins, otherwise the terms decide (`deriveDueDate`), *On Receipt* terms read as on receipt; the print shows *Terms* only when set; both dates are formatted (`fmtDate`) rather than printed as `2026-09-15`. The print, the on-screen preview and the email read the one function.

- **★ C449 — THE INVOICE A CUSTOMER RECEIVES WAS UNSTYLED: ITS TEMPLATE USED THE APP'S CSS VARIABLES IN A WINDOW THAT HAS NO STYLESHEET (2026-09-15).** `window.open("")` + `document.write(html)` gives a blank document; `var(--sc-gold)`, `var(--sc-text)`, `var(--sc-border)`, `var(--sc-text-mut)` resolve to nothing there. The invoice number lost its colour, the table header its background, every row its border, the address its grey — the customer got a plainer document than anyone had designed, and the printed monthly report the same. Literal colours in both print templates; a test slices each template and refuses any `var(--`.

- **C448 — ONE MORE RAW DATABASE SENTENCE ON AN OWNER TOAST, UNDER A DIFFERENT VARIABLE NAME (2026-09-15).** `C415`'s census keyed on `r.error`/`res.error`; the cost-spread rollback toast appended `${reason}` — the schedule insert's raw error, passed in as an argument. Routed; pinned.

- **C447 — "PERMANENTLY DELETE … THIS REMOVES IT FROM THE DATABASE PERMANENTLY" OVER A SOFT DELETE WITH UNDO (2026-09-15).** The contract card's confirmation (`ContractsView`, reviewer-side) described the opposite of what the button does — `softDeleteContracts` stamps `deleted_at` and offers Undo (`C362`). Found by the `C446` grep for "permanently". Reworded to the record.

- **★ C446 — THE PRIVACY PAGE PROMISED "PERMANENT REMOVAL" THAT NOTHING PERFORMS (2026-09-15). ▶ OPERATOR TO REVIEW.** *"Deleted entries are soft-deleted … before permanent removal"* — §11's own warning, written weeks ago: *"a retention promise must be defined AND implemented before it is quoted to anyone."* No purge exists in `src/` or the edge functions. The paragraph now says what happens — soft-deleted, out of the books and reports, kept in the audit trail, restorable — and that we do not currently purge automatically; a test refuses the purge wording while no purge exists in the codebase.

- **★★ C445 — THE PRIVACY PAGE NAMED A PROCESSOR THAT DOES NOT EXIST AND OMITTED THREE THAT DO (2026-09-15). ▶ OPERATOR TO REVIEW — it is legal copy.** *"Processors and sharing"* listed Supabase, Anthropic and **Stripe** — which is integrated nowhere (two comments in the code mention it as an example vendor). It did not name **Sentry** (receives the signed-in account's email, the company id and error context), **Vercel** (hosts the app; access logs) or **Google** (`C395`'s supplier logos are fetched by the browser from `google.com/s2/favicons?domain=…`, so Google sees which suppliers' domains a user views — nothing about the account, but a fact the page should state). Rewritten to what the code does, with a test that pins each claim against the code (`setUser` sends `id`+`email`; `sendDefaultPii` off; the favicon endpoint), the payment line says there are no paid plans yet, and the effective date moved. **A privacy policy is the §9 rule applied to a lawyer's sentence: it must describe the record, and the record is the code.** **One more for the operator's eye, NOT changed:** the Terms describe the service as *"with CPA oversight"* and *"CPA review of accounting methodology"* — true of the accountant-led model, and since `O131`/`C272` a solo owner can sign their own months with no reviewer at all. Whether the Terms should say so is a product-and-legal call.

- **C444 — THE CHAT'S SUGGESTED QUESTIONS SAID "BURN RATE" AND "P&L" (2026-09-15).** A suggested question teaches the person what the product expects them to say. *"How much am I spending each month?" · "Which bills haven't I paid?" · "Did I make money this month?" · "Does anything need my attention?"* — and `ChatComposer`, `ErrorBoundary` and `CompanySwitcher` join the embedded-component jargon scan.

- **★ C443 — THE MONTHLY REPORT PANEL ON REPORTS SAID "RECEIVABLES · PAYABLES · MONTHLY BURN · RUNWAY", ON SCREEN AND IN THE PRINTED PDF (2026-09-15).** `MonthlyReportsPanel` renders inside the client's Reports screen and was in neither the client-screen map nor the embedded-component list of the jargon guard (`C315`/`C340`) — the same gap `C330` found for the detail panel. Its four stat labels, its printed report's labels and heading, and its subtitle (*"computed live from the ledger … ties to your dashboard and P&L"*) now use the vocabulary `C377` gave Home: *Spending each month · Months of cash at this pace · Owed to you · You owe*. Added to the guard's list, which found the subtitle I had not.
