- **★ C442 — THE REVIEWER'S "ALL CLEAR — NOTHING NEEDS REVIEW" GATED ON TWO OF THE FIVE READS IT DEPENDS ON (2026-09-15).** `O98`'s fix on Review gated the all-clear on the completeness check and the statement exceptions; the anomaly, sign-off and intake reads (`C410`, `C418`) each now carry a verdict, and Review read none of them — a failed anomaly read would have shown the CPA the green card. The all-clear branch sits behind all five now, the same *"we couldn't finish checking just now"* card for any of them.

- **C441 — A CUSTOM RANGE WITH ITS START AFTER ITS END READ AS "$0.00" WITH NO EXPLANATION (2026-09-15).** Seen while rewriting the range filter: the two date inputs accept any order, an inverted pair matches nothing, and every figure went to zero silently. One sentence under the inputs now, rendered both ways.

- **★★★ C440 — THE FIRST OF EVERY MONTH FELL INTO THE PREVIOUS MONTH ON REPORTS, FROM ANY US TIMEZONE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, TWO REDS — RUN UNDER `TZ=America/Chicago`.** `ReportsView`'s range filter built `new Date(inv.date)` from the `YYYY-MM-DD` string and read `.getMonth()`. That string parses as UTC MIDNIGHT, which from Chicago is 7 pm the evening before: **`new Date("2026-09-01").getMonth()` is 7 — August.** So on *"This month"*, *"Last month"* and every quarter, an entry dated the 1st belonged to the month before — and rent is dated the 1st. Not a display slip: the P&L, the Balance Sheet's period figures, the By Vendor and By Category reports and `C379`'s plain paragraph all read `filtered`. **`C290` swept the date HELPERS, proved them, and recorded the rule — this filter never called one.** Decided on the string now (`inReportRange`, pure); the test demonstrates the old expression's answer under a US zone rather than asserting it, and the mutation restoring the getter logic dies only when run under that zone — which is the point `C290` made about CI in UTC.

- **C439 — ONE UTC DATE SLICE REMAINED, IN RECONCILE'S CSV DATE FALLBACK (2026-09-15).** `C290` proved the helpers and recorded the rule; `normDate`'s last resort was `new Date(s).toISOString().slice(0,10)` — the exact mistake the helper's own comment warns against, off by a day from any zone ahead of UTC. `ymdLocal` now, and a guard asserts `src/` carries no UTC date slice.

- **★ C438 — THE SENT-INVOICE LIST SHOWED THE PRE-TAX AMOUNT, AND THE LEGACY MARK-PAID PATH COLLECTED IT (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C437`'s class swept through the same screen: the list's amount and the no-ledger-entry mark-paid path summed line items only. `invoiceTotalOf(inv)` = lines + `tax_amount`, to the cent; both read it. *(The A/R entry itself was always right — `C267`'s builder credits the tax to Sales Tax Payable — so "Money owed to you" disagreed with the list by exactly the tax.)*

- **★★ C437 — THE CUSTOMER'S EMAIL SAID THE PRE-TAX SUBTOTAL AS THE AMOUNT DUE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Reading the email body an owner sends with an invoice: *"Please find invoice INV-0004 for $1,200.00 … Total due: $1,200.00"* — `fmt(subtotal)` twice — while the invoice attached showed **Total Due $1,299.00** with sales tax. A customer paying what the email said would have short-paid by the tax, and the audit row (`invoice_sent … $1,200.00`) recorded the smaller figure too. **§9 on the one sentence a third party reads: the description was computed from a different field than the record.** The email reads `total`, itemises *Subtotal* and *Sales tax (x%)* when there is one, and the audit row agrees with the invoice.

- **C436 — "1 ACCOUNTS", "1 EVENTS", "1 CATEGORIES" (2026-09-15).** A rendered probe of every owner screen for plural agreement (React SSR's `<!-- -->` between text nodes had to be stripped first, or every `{n} bill{s}` read as a slip). `plural(n, word, pluralWord)` in `format.js`; the Categories headings, the Audit trail's counts and Home's drill subtitles read it.

- **★ C435 — "SEND 1099-NEC FORMS TO YOUR CONTRACTORS" EVERY JANUARY, TO A COMPANY WITH NO CONTRACTOR (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C434`'s sibling: the three 1099 filing deadlines fired regardless of whether the 1099 plan named anyone. They wait only when `plan1099ForYear(previous year).outstanding > 0` now. **And the plan's assembly moved out of `TaxView` into the lib** — the vendor-key map and the role lookup that `C316` fixed lived in the component; Home and the bell needed the same answer, and a second assembly would have been the `C316` mismatch waiting to recur. One builder, three readers; the `C316` pins now point at the builder. **The gate earned its place on this commit:** the first attempt read `contacts` and `CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS` inside `HomeWaitingList` — its own `useERP` destructure, `C388`'s lesson — and Home would have crashed on load; `noUndefinedRefs` and the render sweep went red and the ship script stopped before the push, which is the difference from `C433` an hour earlier.

- **★ C434 — A BRAND-NEW COMPANY'S FIRST ITEM ON HOME WAS "PAY YOUR 3RD-QUARTER ESTIMATED TAXES — DUE IN 12 DAYS" (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Walking the first-run experience: the deadline calendar fires regardless of income, so a company with no books — or any company in a loss year — was told to PAY an estimated tax every quarter, as a NOW item, with no amount beside it because the estimate was zero. `O122`'s rule on a card that recurs every quarter. `deadlineIsWaiting(d, estimate)`: an ESTIMATED-payment deadline waits only when the estimate is above zero; a FILING deadline (1099s, the return) always waits, since it is about a form. Home's list and the bell read the same function.

- **★ C433 — THE MANUAL-ENTRY FORM BOOKED TWICE ON A DOUBLE CLICK (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The form clears only after the write lands (`C362`, correctly), which is exactly the window in which a second click runs the whole booking again. Gated through the shared `moneyMoves` keyed in-flight (`C402`) under `"manual-entry"`. **And this commit was PUSHED WITH A TEST RED** — `C362`'s pin anchored on the old function name, my chained command printed the FAIL and committed anyway because nothing gated on it. The shipped code was correct and the fix followed in minutes; recorded because §6 says a push is a release, and *"suite green"* is a gate only when the command actually stops on red. *(Reviewer-only screen — `add` is not a client view — but a CPA double-booking an entry is the same defect.)*

- **★ C432 — A DOUBLE CLICK ON "SEND INVOICE →" SENT TWO INVOICES (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C401`–`C403` gated fourteen handlers and the clarification card; the owner's invoice was not among them. Two concurrent runs of `sendInvoice` each minted an id (`draft.id || Date.now()+Math.random()`), booked an A/R entry, stored a row and sent an email. `makeOneInFlight` runs it once; both buttons are disabled and read *"Sending…"* meanwhile — the effect is visible, per `O123`.

- **★★ C431 — RENUMBERING A CATEGORY MADE ITS BALANCE READ $0 UNTIL THE NEXT RELOAD (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `persistAccountEdit` reloaded the chart and nothing else. Every ledger row carries its category's NAME and CODE as a snapshot from the join at load time (`flattenJournalEntries`), so after a rename every transaction, report and the Vendors tab kept the old name — and after a RENUMBER, role-keyed derivations (`glAccountBalance(getAccountByRole(...).code)`) summed rows by the NEW code and found none: the Balance Sheet line for that account read $0, the old code's rows sat under no account, and the equation still balanced because both halves were missing. A page reload fixed it, which is exactly how a wrong figure survives. The ledger reloads after the chart does, only after the write landed.

- **★★ C430 — A STAGED AI DELETE SURVIVED A COMPANY SWITCH, AND CONFIRM WOULD HAVE REMOVED THE PREVIOUS COMPANY'S ENTRIES WHILE THE SCREEN SHOWED THE NEXT ONE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C410`'s census covered loaders; this is the states a PERSON leaves in flight. `pendingAIActions` (the CR-9 confirmation card for a destructive chat action), `pendingImportFile` (a file routed to a view-local importer), `reconcileOffer` (a statement offered to Reconcile) and `shadowResult` were not in `resetCompanyState`. The card carries the old company's entry ids; the writer is a member there, so RLS permits it; `Confirm` would have deleted them and then reloaded the new company as if nothing happened. The offer is the `O139` shape from the client: a reconciliation for one company's statement written under another's id. All four cleared; the guard finds every `pending*`/`*Offer` state by name so the next one is added on purpose.

- **★ C429 — REPORTS SAID "NO MONTH HAS BEEN SIGNED OFF — THESE FIGURES HAVEN'T BEEN REVIEWED BY ANYONE" AND "YOU BROUGHT IN $0.00" FOR THE LENGTH OF EVERY LOAD (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** The `C428` flash on the one screen where the words are claims: `signoffsLoadOk` started `true`, so during the in-flight read `[]` read as *no month signed*, and the period paragraph computed over an empty ledger. `signoffsLoadOk` starts **null** (unknown) and is reset to null on a switch; Reports says *"Checking which months have been signed off…"* and *"Loading your figures…"* until the reads land. `ownerTrust` reads `!== false`, since Home's panel already shows its own loading state.

- **★ C428 — "NO VENDORS YET" FLASHED FOR THE LENGTH OF EVERY LOAD (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C409` closed the failed read; the LOADING read had the same shape one second at a time — every records screen rendered its empty state between mount and the company's data arriving, an empty result claimed before the query answered. The seven screens render *"Loading your suppliers…"* while `companyDataLoaded` is false, ahead of the empty state; pinned per screen in the `C409` test alongside the failed and clean-empty cases. *(Home already gated its checklist on the same flag — this is that gate, on the screens that share its data.)*

- **★★ C427 — RELOADING ON THE SETTINGS SCREEN SEEDED THE FORM FROM THE RESET DEFAULTS, AND A SAVE WOULD HAVE WRITTEN THE BLANKS (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The settings draft was seeded on entering the view (`[view]`), which on a reload-while-on-Settings runs before `loadAllData` resolves: `companySettings` is still `{ name: "", address: "" … }` from the reset, the draft copies it, the real settings arrive a second later into a state the draft no longer reads — and the form shows a blank company name over a company that has one. `C247` made the save honest about landing; it would have landed blanks. Seeded only once `companyDataLoaded` is true. **And the first placement threw at render** — `companyDataLoaded` is declared 1,500 lines below the effect, so its dep sat in the temporal dead zone; `noTdzInHookDeps` (`C318`) caught it before the build did.

- **★ C426 — A SCREEN'S OWN STATE SURVIVED A COMPANY SWITCH (2026-09-15).** The view container was keyed by `view` alone, so staying on Transactions while switching companies kept `BooksView` mounted with its bulk selection (`picked`, `C318`), its search, its open edit form — three transactions ticked on one company read *"3 selected"* over the next one's table, with ids that belong to nothing on it. `resetCompanyState` clears App-level state; it cannot reach a view's `useState`. Keyed by `view:companyId` now, so a switch remounts the screen — `C297`'s locality rule, applied to the whole view.

- **★★ C425 — TWO OF TODAY'S RENDER TESTS IMPORTED A FIXTURE FROM A MODULE THAT DOES NOT EXPORT IT, AND RENDERED AN EMPTY CONTEXT WHILE CLAIMING A POPULATED ONE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `import { POPULATED } from "./helpers/renderView.jsx"` — the fixture lives in `populatedFixture.js`. Under the test transform the binding was silently `undefined`, `{ ...undefined }` is a no-op, and `C410`/`C417`'s rendered assertions passed against no rows because they happened not to need any. **The build catches a missing export in `src/`; nothing built the tests.** Found by a probe (every view with every load failed, both seats — clean) that crashed on `POPULATED.navSeat` before it could run. `tests/testImportsResolve.test.js` now checks every line-start named import in `tests/` against the module's exports (extensionless paths resolved; a quoted import inside an assertion is not an import). **The tenth mechanical slip of the same family: an edit that did not do what it was written to do, caught by a check that LOOKED rather than by care.**

- **★ C424 — DISMISSING "ADD YOUR ACCOUNTANT" ON ONE COMPANY DISMISSED IT ON EVERY COMPANY THIS BROWSER OPENS (2026-09-15).** A census of storage keys for company scoping: every other per-company key carries the id (`cfai_tax_<cid>`, `cfai_mergeDismissed_<cid>`, `C389`'s declined-recurring key); this one was device-wide, read once at mount and never on a switch. Scoped and re-read per company.

- **★ C423 — "A/R" AND "A/P" WERE NOT IN THE JARGON BAR, SO "A/R booked ✓" PASSED IT ON THREE OWNER TOASTS AND A BUTTON (2026-09-15).** `OWNER_JARGON_RE` names *receivables* and *payables* (`C314` widened them to the plural) and never the abbreviations a bookkeeper actually types. Widened to `\bA\/[RP]\b`; the guards immediately found *"Mark Paid → Collect A/R"*, *"Invoice 1042 sent … — A/R booked ✓"* (twice) and an audit sentence. *"recorded as money owed to you"* now — the sidebar's own words for the same thing (`C314`, `C320`: one vocabulary). **A regex-shaped bar catches the vocabulary it was given** (`C320`'s note on *"AR Management"*), and this is the third time a form of the same word walked past it.

- **★ C422 — `$4625.00` ON THE INVOICE A CUSTOMER RECEIVES (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** A census of `$${x.toFixed(2)}` found sixteen; the owner-facing ones were the invoice HTML a customer is sent (line amounts, subtotal, tax, total), the duplicate card an owner answers (*"already booked on … for $1234.56"*, two sites), the off-rhythm card, and the vendor-creep sentence's default money. All through `fmtMoney` now. Journal memos, audit details and the matcher's reasoning keep the bare form — they are the accountant's, and the owner's Audit trail scrubs (`C408`). The guard excuses by CALL SITE (`logAudit(`, `memo:`) rather than by file, so a new owner sentence in a lib fails.

- **★ C421 — THE SIGN-IN SCREEN SHOWED THE AUTH SERVICE'S OWN SENTENCE (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `C415`'s rule on the first screen a client ever sees: both auth forms set `e.message` verbatim. Supabase's are mostly readable and some say what is wrong without what to do — *"Email not confirmed"* — and *"Failed to fetch"* is the browser talking. `authErrorCopy` maps the handful auth actually returns to a sentence with the next step (*"Confirm your email first — there's a link in the message we sent you"*), passes plain sentences through, and replaces code-shaped text.

- **★★ C420 — A FAILED MEMBERSHIP READ OPENED "CREATE YOUR COMPANY" (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `loadCompanies`, the root's first read after sign-in, dropped `error`: `[]` from a failed read and `[]` from a brand-new signup are the same value, and `if (cos.length === 0) setShowCompanySetup(true)` opens setup on it — so a returning owner on a bad connection was one click from creating a SECOND company, with their real one still there behind a read that never ran. `O98` at the root, and the one instance whose consequence is new data rather than a wrong sentence. The failure is recorded and a retry screen renders ahead of setup — *"this isn't a sign that you don't have one"* — with *Try again* re-running the read; setup opens only on a read that ran and found nothing.

- **★★ C419 — COMPANY SETUP CHECKED ITS FIRST STEP AND FIRED THE OTHER THREE (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The first minute of a client's life: `create_company` was checked; `seed_company_accounts`, the starting bank account and the trial subscription were **awaited and never read**. A failed seed — the exact thing `O108`'s unrun check (c) worried about after `069` revoked `anon` — produced a company with NO chart, `onComplete` fired anyway, and the owner landed on Home looking at the built-in chart as theirs (`CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS` falls back for display); the first booking would have materialised every account with `system_role NULL`, every role lookup falling back forever. **And a retry after any failure would have created a SECOND company**, because `create_company` ran first every time. Each step is checked now, **the seed is read back** (an RPC returning without an error and the chart existing are two facts), each later step is skipped when its row already exists, and the created company is remembered so *"press Create again"* resumes from the failed step. Raw messages route through `plainWriteError` (`C415`).

- **★ C418 — "NOTHING NEEDS YOUR ATTENTION" WAS SAID OVER A FAILED ANOMALY READ (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The last of the trust header's inputs without a verdict: `loadAnomalies` dropped `error`, a failed read left the rows empty, the scan correctly declined to run over an unloaded set (`anomaliesLoadedRef`) — and the header reached all-clear on `[]`, the `C194` shape on the surface whose whole job is to be trusted. `anomaliesChecked` joins `completenessChecked` and `signoffsChecked`: the *correct* line says *"We couldn't check for unusual activity just now"*, the `noAnomalies` net is false, all-clear is unreachable, and an open question still comes first. **Every read the trust header depends on now records whether it ran** (documents, sign-offs, anomalies), which is what `O98` asked for on the day it was written.

- **★ C417 — THE TAXES SCREEN SHOWED $0 OF ESTIMATED PAYMENTS OVER A FAILED READ, AND A TAB THROUGH THE INPUT WOULD HAVE SAVED THAT 0 OVER THE REAL FIGURE (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `C409`'s question asked of the screens that read for themselves: `TaxView` loads `tax_settings` in its own effect, and a failed read fell through to a device-local mirror or to `estPaid: 0` — so *"Estimated Payments Made $0.00"* and a *"Still Owed"* inflated by the same amount, and since the input's `onBlur` saves whatever it holds, leaving the field would have upserted 0 over the payments the person had actually recorded. The verdict is recorded (`loadOk`); while false the box carries the notice, the input is disabled, `save` refuses first, **and the local mirror stands in only for a company with no row yet — never for a read that failed**, where it could be stale against the figure the database holds.

- **★★★ C416 — THE CHART OF ACCOUNTS HAD NO RESET ON SWITCH, NO STALE GUARD AND NO VERDICT — A BOOKING COULD HAVE REFERENCED THE LAST COMPANY'S ACCOUNT IDS (2026-09-15). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS — ONE AFTER ITS PIN STOPPED PASSING ON THE WRONG RESET.** `C413`'s question asked of the one load that lives outside `App.jsx`: `useAccounts` kept the previous company's accounts until the next company's read resolved, and forever if it failed. In that window every role resolved against the LAST company's chart, and `post_journal_entry` would have accepted lines pointing at another tenant's account ids — **the lines table's RLS checks its own `company_id`, not the account's** — a cross-tenant reference reachable by switching companies quickly on a slow connection. On a FAILED first read the chart was empty, every role fell through to the built-in fallback, and `ensureAccount` would have materialised the built-in chart onto the company: `O108` finding 4, the absorber, reached through a network blip. And `CHART_OF_ACCOUNTS` falls back to the built-in chart for DISPLAY, so the Categories screen showed it as the company's own, with edit and delete controls over accounts that do not exist. The hook clears on switch, drops a late result through a per-render ref, records `loadOk`; **both write paths refuse before any business rule while it is false** (`CHART_NOT_LOADED`, audited), and the Categories screen renders the notice instead of the built-in chart. **The first M1 survived: removing the reset left the `!companyId` early return's own `setAccounts([])` to satisfy a pin that only asked "before the await"** — sharpened to sit between `setLoading(true)` and the read.

- **★★ C415 — TWENTY-SEVEN TOASTS ENDED WITH THE DATABASE'S OWN SENTENCE (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** `C414`'s class swept across every toast: `${r?.error}` on the tail of *"We couldn't save Sysco's details — nothing was changed."* rendered *new row violates row-level security policy for table "contacts"* to an owner on the vendor form — and *duplicate key value violates unique constraint "…"*, *update matched 0 rows*, *PGRST116: JSON object requested…* on the others. True, and useless: the person has no move. The checked-write helpers return the raw message on purpose (the console and the audit trail need it); `plainWriteError` (pure) maps the messages a write actually returns to the one thing the person can do — *"You don't have permission to change this — ask an admin"*, *"There's already one with that name"*, *"We couldn't find that record any more — reload"*, *"The connection dropped"*, *"Your sign-in has expired"*, *"Something on our side needs fixing — nothing you typed is wrong"* — passes the `078` trigger's sentence through untouched (it was already the owner's), and **OMITS anything it does not recognise rather than quoting it**, since a sentence that looks like code reads as broken. Twenty-four tails routed; the four whose template read *"Couldn't bring that back: unknown"* reshaped; Admin excepted (platform-admin only). The census refuses to pass vacuously (it must still find the tails, routed).

- **★★ C414 — THE CHAT PRINTED THE RAW ERROR AND AN OPERATOR'S HINT INTO THE OWNER'S BUBBLE, AND THE CLASSIFIER MISSED THE BROWSER'S OWN WORDING FOR A DROPPED CONNECTION (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** The chat's catch rendered *"⚠ I couldn't complete that request."* followed by `e.message` verbatim — `AI service error (429 Too Many Requests): {"error":"rate_limited",…}` — and hints written for the operator (*"check that the ai-proxy edge function is deployed"*, *"verify the ai-proxy model configuration"*). `classifyAIFailure` already knew which of four things had happened and carried an owner sentence on the thrown error (`C269`); the chat never read it, and its sentences were about DOCUMENTS (*"we can't read documents right now"*), the wrong subject for a typed question. `chatFailureCopy` gives the same kinds chat-shaped sentences (*"I've hit my hourly limit for answering — it clears in about 12 minutes. Your question is still in the box"*), the catch declines the send so the composer keeps the text (`C404`), clears the loading state before returning — a `return` inside the catch would have left the chat spinning forever — and the operator detail goes to an `ai_chat_failed` audit row's meta. **And writing its test found a classifier hole on the UPLOAD path: the network pattern matched Node's `fetch failed` and not the browser's `Failed to fetch`**, so a connection blip mid-upload classified UNKNOWN — permanent — and held the document for a person to look at, when the drain would have retried it a minute later. Widened, pinned as `retryable`.

- **★★★ C413 — THE MID-SWITCH GUARD COMPARED A PROP TO ITSELF AND HAD NEVER ONCE FIRED (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** Found by asking `C410`'s question of the async loads: which per-company reads can land a stale result? `loadAllData` reads `const cid = currentCompany.id` and later `if (currentCompany.id !== cid) return;` — **but `currentCompany` is a PROP captured when the closure was created, and a prop does not change inside one closure.** So the CR-19 guard on the ledger, the guard after the settled reads, and the `finally` that marks the company loaded (**added by `C277`, with a note that it was "now conditional on still being on that company"** — a guard I wrote that could never be false) were all tautologies: switch companies while a slow load is in flight and the previous company's ledger, contacts and documents land over the new one, after `resetCompanyState` has run. **`C195(7)` in its purest form — a guard whose condition cannot be true reads exactly like one that is holding — and `tests/loadBatching.test.js` pinned the expression verbatim, proving the guard was WRITTEN.** `refreshMailState` had the same shape. A ref assigned every render (`currentCompanyIdRef`) read through `stillOn(cid)` replaces all of them; and the six helper loaders that set state after their own await (anomalies, notifications, filed deadlines, comments, contracts, statement exceptions) ask it too, because a late result for a company we have left is the same bleed one table at a time. The guard asserts no `currentCompany.id !== cid` survives anywhere in `App.jsx`.

- **★ C412 — THE SEND-INVOICE BUTTON REFUSED ON CLICK; NOW IT SAYS WHAT IS MISSING AND WAITS (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS — ONE AFTER ITS GUARD LEARNED THERE ARE TWO BUTTONS.** A sweep of owner screens for controls that refuse on click (`O124`) found one: *Send Invoice →* was enabled over an empty draft and answered *"Add a customer name first."* in a toast. `invoiceSendBlockers` (pure) names the three things the handler checks — customer name, email, a line with an amount — the screen lists them under *"Before you can send:"*, both Send buttons (form and preview) are disabled while any remain, and the handler's guard is the same function, so the list and the refusal cannot disagree. **The first mutation survived because the replace hit the PREVIEW button, which SSR never renders** (`showPreview` is state) — the render test was true of the one button it could see; the second site is held by count in the source.

- **★★ C410 — A COMPANY SWITCH NEVER CLEARED THE SIGN-OFFS, AND A FAILED SIGN-OFF READ SAID "NOBODY HAS REVIEWED THESE" (2026-09-15). SIX MUTATIONS, SIX REDS.** `C409`'s question, asked of the loads that do NOT go through `loadAllData`: the sign-off/intake effect runs per company with `if (so.ok) setSignoffs(...)` and no record of whether it ran, so a failed read left `[]` — the same value as a company with nothing signed — and Reports' attestation line (`C343`, built so a report *"can never sound surer than the books"*) said *"No month has been signed off yet — these figures haven't been reviewed by anyone"*, the trust panel *"Awaiting your accountant's sign-off"*: `O98`, on the two sentences whose whole job is to say what has been reviewed. **And `resetCompanyState` never touched `signoffs`, `intakeRows`, `hasAttester` or `cutoffDate`** — §3's *"switching companies calls `resetCompanyState()` so no previous company's data bleeds into the next"* was true of the tables `loadAllData` read in June and false of every load added since, so between a switch and the new company's read resolving — or for good, if it failed — the screen carried the LAST company's signed months (and `signedPeriodForDate` HELD this company's entries dated into them), its held questions on Home, its attester, and its Day One over this company's bookings. A census (`loaders whose body filters on company_id` × `setters they write` − `setters in the reset`) found exactly those, plus `setStatementExceptionsLoadFailed`; it is the standing guard now, and the effect's setters are asserted separately since it is not a named loader. `signoffsLoadOk` is recorded on the same pass; `ownerTrustState` takes `signoffsChecked` and cannot reach all-clear without it; the Reports line and the trust line say *"we couldn't check which months have been signed off"* rather than describing the query as the books.

- **★ C411 — REPORTS CALLED A SOLO OWNER'S OWN SIGN-OFF AN ACCOUNTANT'S REVIEW (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, TWO REDS.** Found reading `C343`'s wiring for `C410`: `ReportsView` passes `selfSigned: ownerTrust?.selfSigned`, and `ownerTrustState` **never returned `selfSigned`** — the input was consumed for the trust panel's own line and dropped. So a solo owner who signed July themselves read *"Reviewed and signed off through July 2026"* on every report — the accountant's sentence, the exact conflation `C272` was built to prevent, one screen over from where it was prevented. `C343`'s test pinned the caller's expression verbatim and never asked whether the field it read existed (`·3a` on a single property). Returned now, false when nothing is signed; rendered both ways.

- **★★ C409 — A FAILED TABLE READ RENDERED AS "NO VENDORS YET" ON EVERY RECORDS SCREEN (2026-09-15). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** `C277` made the twelve secondary loads `allSettled` and recorded, deliberately, that *"a rejected read yields `{}` so every consumer takes the same 'no data' branch"*. One layer up that is `O98` on every screen a client opens to look at their own records: a failed `contacts` read drew *"No vendors yet — vendors appear automatically when you upload invoices"*, a failed `documents` read *"No documents yet"*, a failed `audit_log` read *"No activity recorded yet"*, `vendor_rules` → *"No rules yet"*, `recurring_transactions`, `ar_invoices` the same. `C250` closed exactly this on the Team screen — *"a silently empty list is the most reassuring possible way to be wrong"* — and named it a class; the other seven screens kept the shape. The ledger fetch had the guard since CR-18 (*"failed ≠ empty"*); the tables around it did not. `settledFailures` (pure) turns the settled array into `{ table: message }` — **a fulfilled response carrying PostgREST's `error` counts**, which is how a bad select fails (`C308`'s column name would have been one) — the failures are recorded on the same pass, cleared on a company switch, and each screen renders `LoadFailedNotice` in place of its empty state: *"We couldn't load your suppliers just now, so this list may be incomplete — it isn't a confirmation that there are none."* The read order is pinned table-for-table against the settled list so a reordered read cannot be named as the wrong table; each screen is rendered both ways (a failed read shows the notice and not the empty state; a clean empty read shows the empty state and not the notice), because *"the notice appears"* is equally satisfied by showing it always.

- **★★ C408 — THE AUDIT TRAIL IS A CLIENT SETTINGS SCREEN, AND ITS ROWS WERE WRITTEN FOR THE CPA (2026-09-15). FIVE MUTATIONS, FIVE REDS.** Started as a probe of the Home Activity feed (does `ownerActivityText` leak audit jargon?) — it does not, because that feed admits only eight actions (`paid|approv|reject|recode|void|flag|info_requested`) and the scrub covers their tails. The real reader was one screen over: `audit` is in `SETTINGS_VIEW_IDS`, inside the client seat, and `AuditView` rendered `entry.detail` raw — so an owner opening Settings → Audit trail read *"Sysco · $824.60 → Food Cost (92% confidence · 2026-08-06)"*, *"(GAAP capitalize)"*, *"recorded as prepaid (1300), amortizing over 12 months"*, *"Capitalized Sabine $4,625.00 — 60mo straight-line"*, *"Created account 6520 … it was not in this company's chart"*. `activityFeed.js`'s own header records that the log *"carries bookkeeping notation on purpose"* — true, and the screen that shows it to an owner was never held to the bar (`C386` rendered its chrome with fixture rows). **Twenty-three sentences failed the bar in a census of every `logAudit` literal in `src/`.** Eleven are rewritten at the writer with the technical field moved to `after_state` (`confidence`, `gaap_type`, the account `code` the O108 detector reads — the sentence names the category, the meta keeps the number); five money strings go through `fmtMoney` (a bare `$2400.00` reads as a chart code to the guard, and as a typo to a person); the owner seat's Audit trail renders through `scrubOwnerActivity` for the trailing notation older rows carry, while the reviewer's seat and the CSV keep the raw detail. **Twelve are excused BY ACTION NAME with the reason** — reconciliation persistence, depreciation stamps, the payroll register's Dr/Cr shape, a refusal that must quote the number the model produced (`C260`) — and a stale excuse fails. `tests/ownerAuditTrail.test.js` is the standing census; the O108 copy test was re-aimed at its property (a query-claim, *"was not in this company's list"*) rather than the word *chart*.

- **★★ C393 — "invoice_booked" WAS WRITTEN BEFORE THE LEDGER ANSWERED, AT SIX SITES (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS — ONE AFTER ITS TEST STOPPED PASSING ON −1.** `C392`'s find, swept as a class: the upload batch (`highConfidence.map(inv => { logAudit(…); return bookToDb(inv); })`) and all four booking branches of the clarification card (*different purchase*, *unsure*, *new charge*, the category answer) wrote the audit row and THEN called the writer. A refused booking — a signed month, the cutoff, an RPC error, the *"Not now"* of `C390` — left *"booked → Food Cost"* in the audit trail with nothing behind it, and enriched the contact for a purchase that was never recorded. `C240` recorded exactly this shape on the delete path; the booking paths had it too. The card's `bookAnswer` takes the audit `{detail, meta}` and writes it after `if (!jeId) return false` — one place, four callers; the batch audits inside `.then(jeId => { if (jeId) … })`. The learning signal (`ai_clarification_learned`) deliberately stays before the write: the answer WAS given whether or not the booking lands. **A mutation swapping the gate for `if (true)` survived because the test compared `indexOf` results and −1 sorts before everything** — the fourth time an index-based structure pin has passed vacuously; the gate index is asserted present first now.

- **★ C392 — THE GAAP CARD AUDITED A BOOKING BEFORE READING WHETHER IT LANDED, AND ITS ROLLBACK SENTENCE DID NOT READ THE ROLLBACK (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** From the *"try again"* census: `applyGaapAnswer` wrote `invoice_booked` to the audit trail and enriched the contact **before** `jeId` was consulted, so a refused capitalization left *"booked → Fixed Assets"* in the one record an accountant is entitled to trust (`C240`'s shape, on the owner's card). And `compensateCapitalization` said *"the capitalization was rolled back … Please try again"* **whatever `comp.ok` was** — when the rollback itself failed, the purchase was in the books with no schedule, and the invitation to retry would have booked it twice (`C245`'s rule: a failure that cannot be retried must never be described as one). Both sentences also carried *"depreciation"* and *"capitalization"* on an owner card. Audit and enrichment sit inside `if (jeId)`; a failed rollback says *"is in your books, but we couldn't set up its cost spread and couldn't undo the entry either. Don't try again — send this to your accountant"* and returns; a successful one says the entry was undone and nothing is in the books.

- **★ C391 — THE HELD-WRITER CENSUS, RUN TO THE END: TWO MORE ROWS NOBODY READ (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** `C390` prompted the question the class deserved from the start: **list every `INTAKE_STATUS.HELD` writer and name its reader.** Eighteen sites. Two had none that a person could reach after a reload: *(1)* `clarificationSettle`'s *"a booking from your answer did not land"* — a person answered the card, the write claimed to book and returned no id, and the row went terminal with nothing on any screen; it is a constant now and reads through the partial-hold door with its own sentence (*"You answered our question about X, but recording it didn't go through"*). *(2)* the client-seat statement stash — `pendingStatementStashes` already lists it on Bank Import, which an owner cannot open, and the owner's trust panel did not count it, so *"everything you sent is accounted for"* stood over a statement waiting for an accountant to add it. It is in `heldForAccountant` now. **The rest are read**: questions (`C365`), unreadable (`C369`), payroll (`C372`), deferred and signed-month (`C373`), partial (`C390`), the catch-all (`C364`, `unknown_documents`), routed and bank-in-review rows (the statement exceptions), duplicates (nothing to do). Recorded as a census so the next HELD writer is added to a list rather than to the class.

- **★★ C390 — A DOCUMENT ONLY PARTLY IN THE BOOKS HAD NO SCREEN AFTER A RELOAD (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS — ONE AFTER THE ASSERTION WAS SHARPENED.** Found by reading what *"Not now"* on the signed-month decision leaves behind: the entry never lands, so `C311`'s gate holds the intake row with *"1 of 3 transaction(s) saved — 2 did not, so this document is not fully recorded"* — a true sentence, written by TWO sites (the batch gate and `settlePartialHold`) in two copies, and **read by nothing**. HELD is terminal, so after a reload the trust panel read *"everything you sent is accounted for"* over a file two-thirds missing, and no card on Home or Review offered to finish it. The same hold arises when a write is refused mid-batch. It is the last branch of the *"held for a person, no screen after a reload"* class `C365`–`C373` closed — the one that was recorded as closed. `partialHoldDetail` is now the one sentence both writers store and `parsePartialHold` reads (**the guard found the second writer**); `heldPartialRows` lists them, Home carries a NOW item with *Bring it back* (`reloadHeldIntake`, under the original intake id), and the trust panel's Documents line says *"only partly in your books"* and its completeness net reads false. **The trust-net mutation survived the first test because it asserted the LINE's state, which the else-if chain sets, and not the NET, which `capturedOk` sets** — two outputs from one input, and the assertion aimed at the one that would have stayed right.

- **★ C389 — "NO THANKS" ON A RECURRING SUGGESTION LASTED UNTIL THE NEXT RELOAD (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION SURVIVED, THEN ONE RED.** `C362` fixed the *"Yes, set it up"* side of Home's recurring card (the rule was never written, so the card came back). The *"No thanks"* side had the same hole from the other direction: the declined set was a ref, so a reload — or a company switch and back — emptied it, the scan found the same pattern, and the card asked again, every month the vendor kept charging. `O122`'s rule, on the answer that was supposed to end the question. Remembered per company on this device (`declinedRecurring.js`, injected storage so the shape is testable; a missing or throwing storage reads as nothing declined) — **per-device, stated as the same trade `C281` made for merge suggestions**: a durable answer wants a column or a profile namespace, a re-shown card on a second device costs one glance, and a *yes* is durable by construction because it becomes a rule. **The first mutation survived: deleting the line that folds the stored keys into the filter left `readDeclinedRecurring(` present and its result read by nothing** — a read nobody consumes is `C238`'s checked-write-nobody-reads, on a read. Pinned as structure: read → folded into the set → the set IS the filter.

- **★★ C388 — "MARK FILED" ON THE TAXES SCREEN CHANGED NOTHING ANYWHERE ELSE (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** Found while reading the bell's tax-deadline generator beside Home's: both computed the next deadline from the calendar alone. `tax_settings.filed_deadlines` — the map a person builds by ticking *Mark filed* — was written and read by `TaxView` and nothing else, so Home's waiting list and the bell went on saying *"Pay 3rd-quarter estimated taxes — due today"* after the person had marked it done, and kept saying it until the date passed. **`O123`'s rule on a tick-box: an action whose effect is invisible will be repeated.** The map is loaded with the company (a failed read leaves it alone — *"could not ask"* is not *"none filed"*), `nextUrgentDeadline` takes it and skips a filed deadline, Home and the bell pass it, and `TaxView` pushes the new map only after the row landed. `filedKey` is one function on both sides, because two spellings of `${key}-${year}` is how the reader would quietly stop matching. **The render sweep caught a crash the build cannot see**: `HomeWaitingList` is its own component with its own `useERP` destructure, and the first edit read `filedDeadlines` there without destructuring it.

- **★ C387 — THE BELL HAD ITS OWN DEFINITION OF "MATCHED TO YOUR BANK" (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Read while fixing `C381`: the bank-match notification counted days from ANY reconciliation row's `created_at || statement_date` — an open or abandoned session counted as a match — and fired on a company with no books at all (*"Your books haven't been matched to your bank yet"* on day one), while Home's list and the trust panel read `bankMatchStatus`, which counts verified completions only and stays silent without books. **Two readings of one fact, on two surfaces a person sees in the same minute** — the §12 forked-definition warning, in the bell. The generator calls the same `bankMatchStatus` on the live refs now; a test pins the call and the two directions the old reading got wrong.

- **★★ C386 — THE SETTINGS SCREENS, RENDERED FOR AN OWNER AND READ (2026-09-15). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** `C378` renamed their headings; nothing had read their bodies. - **★ C385 — "SPENDING IS UP 804% VERSUS LAST MONTH" NAMED A MONTH IT DID NOT COMPARE (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** Seen in `C384`'s render: the fixture has expenses in January and March and nothing in February, and Home read *"Spending is up 804% versus last month"*. `C377` fixed the partial-month half (complete months only, a $250 floor); the other half remained — *"the previous month"* was **the previous month WITH DATA**, so a company with a quiet month, or one whose statement had not been uploaded yet, was told a trend against a month two or three back under a sentence that says *last month*. A gap is not a baseline. It compares the calendar month immediately before the last complete one, or says nothing; the year boundary is pinned so December → January still reads as adjacent.

Now *"Leases and ongoing agreements"*, the type's label, and *"there isn't a month of cash on hand to cover it"*. **The rows behind the block's Show toggle cannot be drawn by an SSR pass** (`C246`'s recorded limit), so the label mapping is pinned as structure and the subtitle by render. Both seats are rendered — one vocabulary.

- **★ C383 — THE SETTINGS SCREENS WERE NEVER IN THE DOOR CHECK, AND ONE HAD A DOOR AN OWNER CANNOT FOLLOW (2026-09-15). ONE MUTATION, ONE RED.** The sweep behind `C381`/`C382` — every direct `setView` to a reviewer-only screen, outside those screens — ended at `QBOImportView`: an owner can open *Import from QuickBooks* (it is in the client's Settings), and dropping a `.qbo` BANK file there said *"opening the bank import instead"* and called `setView("bank")`, which the route guard bounced Home. `C315`'s guard should have said so and could not: its `CLIENT_SCREENS` map listed the eight nav-row screens and **none of the ten Settings screens, though `SETTINGS_VIEW_IDS` sits inside `CLIENT_VIEW_IDS`** — a guard scoped to where the walls had just moved (`C312`'s *"a test scoped to where you already looked"*). The drop now goes through `routeFileToType`, the router that already knows both seats (a reviewer lands on Bank Import; an owner has the file saved for their accountant and is told so), and check (1) covers the Settings screens — the other nine passed on first run, so the QuickBooks door was the only one.

- **★ C382 — THE CHAT SAID "Opened recon" TO AN OWNER, AND THE ROUTE GUARD THEN UNDID IT (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** `C381`'s class on the next shared door: the AI `navigate` action mapped the model's word to a view id and called `setView` bare, so *"reconcile my bank"* from an owner put *"Opened recon"* in the reply — a route id, and a claim — and the guard bounced them Home a render later with *"your accountant looks after that part"*. The action asks `canSeeView` with the live seat before any `setView`: a screen this seat may not open lands Home and the reply says whose it is; a screen that does not exist says *"I couldn't find a screen by that name"* rather than *opened*; a screen that opens is named by `viewLabel` — read off the sidebar's own rows, so the chat and the nav cannot call one screen two things — and **every view the router can render is required to have a name**, which found `dashboard`, `contracts` and `legal` with none. Pinned as structure (the action loop is not importable): the gate precedes the first `setView`, the refusal branch contains no *Opened*.

- **★★ C381 — THE BELL TOLD THE OWNER TO RUN A BANK MATCH, AND THE CLICK TOLD THEM IT WASN'T THEIRS (2026-09-15). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** Found by asking which doors out of Home were NOT the U1 list: the notification bell. `notifications` is ONE stored list per company, written by whichever session generated the row and read by every seat on it — and five generators wrote the accountant's instruction with the accountant's door (*"Run a quick bank match…"* → `recon`; *"Open Bank Import to pick the account…"* → `bank`; *"Open Review to resolve."* → `review`; the signed-month and depreciation holds → `review`). `openNotification` was a bare `setView(n.link_view)`, so an owner who did what the alert said was bounced Home by the route guard with *"Your accountant looks after that part"* — **the alert says do it, the tap says it isn't yours, and the same fact sits one screen away on Home in the owner's words with the owner's action** (U1: *"drop your latest statement and we'll do it"*). `O124`'s rule on the bell: a control that refuses on click teaches you that clicking is how you find out. The door is resolved AT CLICK TIME for the seat that clicked (`notificationTarget`, reading the same view list `navRedirect` reads, so a gated link lands Home with no bounce and the accountant's link still opens Reconcile), the five sentences are written for whoever reads them (no *"Open Review"*, no *"bank match"*, no *"depreciation"* — a stored sentence has no seat gate), and the empty state's *"taxes, anomalies, and reviews"* is *"taxes, unusual activity, and things to check"*. A guard reads every `createNotification` literal off the source: the link is a routable view, Home or a `txn:` template, and no title or description names a reviewer screen or fails the owner bar. **The txn: branch and the accountant's doors are pinned beside the owner's, because "the owner lands Home" is equally satisfied by sending everyone Home.**

- **★★ C363 — THE REVIEW SCREEN'S OWN "POST" BUTTONS WROTE NOTHING TO THE LEDGER (2026-09-14). FIVE MUTATIONS, FIVE REDS.** The last of the ✓ audit. A reload dropped the entry, and since the unknown-document record is itself in-session (`O97`'s class — the record, its draft and its watch triggers never reach the `unknown_documents` table; only the intake row and the bytes are durable), the document came back unposted too. Both await the write now and mark the document posted only on the ledger's answer.

- **★★ C362 — FIVE MORE ✓ SENTENCES NOW READ THE WRITE THEY DESCRIBE, AND ONE OF THEM HAD NO WRITE (2026-09-14). EIGHT MUTATIONS, EIGHT REDS.** The same audit as `C360`/`C361`, carried through the rest of the list.

Don't post this entry again."*
  - **★★★ AND THE UNCHECKED-WRITE GUARD HAD EXCUSED `contracts` WITH A REASON THAT WAS FALSE FOR THIS TABLE'S MOST IMPORTANT WRITE.** *"A failed edit leaves the contract visibly unchanged on screen"* — true of the edit form, untrue of the marker, which is painted BEFORE the write. **An excuse is a claim about every write on the table, and it was checked against one.** Removed; the guard immediately surfaced a second unchecked write on the same table.

Now: checked per row, the screen and the audit trail cover only what landed, a partial failure names the count still in place, and a total failure offers no Undo.

- **★★★ C361 — EVERY EDIT TO A CONTACT LOADED FROM THE DATABASE HAS BEEN SILENTLY DROPPED SINCE 2026-06-06, AND REPORTED AS SAVED (2026-09-14). EIGHT MUTATIONS, EIGHT REDS.** Found by continuing the ✓-toast audit into `Tax1099View` — *"Vendor saved ✓"* fired beside an unawaited `persistContact` — and asking what that function returns for a loaded row.
  - **★★★ ONE MISSING FIELD, ONE UNIQUE INDEX, ONE `ignoreDuplicates`.** `persistContact` decides UPDATE-vs-UPSERT on `contact.db_id`. **The load mapping never set it — from the first commit** — so every contact loaded from the database carried `id` (the uuid) and no `db_id`, and every edit to one took the UPSERT path. Before 2026-06-06 that was a bare `insert`, which the `(company_id, name_key)` index refused **loudly**. On 2026-06-06 the insert became `upsert(…, { ignoreDuplicates: true })` — `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` — for the concurrent-upload race, and from that day the refusal became a silent no-op: the conflict returned no row, the function fetched the OLD row to recover its id, and **returned `ok: true` with the old data.** A contact created in THIS session got `db_id` stamped when its insert resolved, which is why the Red River drives never saw it — every vendor was minutes old.
  - **★★★ THE BLAST RADIUS IS EVERY CONTACT FORM IN THE PRODUCT, AFTER ANY RELOAD:** a vendor's payment terms, email, phone, website, notes, tags · **an O111 alias** — the merge a person asserts by hand, and `C281`'s *"now counts as ✓"* was gated on exactly the `ok` this path returned falsely, so the guard written to prevent a false merge success was defeated by the layer beneath it · **a 1099 business type and "marked 1099 sent"** — the surface that decides what is filed with the IRS under the accountant's name, where a CPA answering *"this vendor is an LLC"* saw the tick and got the same `NEEDS_INFO` question after a reload, **a category-1 card by `O122`'s rule** · an invoice-driven enrichment of an existing contact, **audited as `contact_updated` while nothing was written.** *(Nothing to repair on live data: the edits were never written, so there is nothing wrong in the table — only absent. Anyone who set one of these since June has to set it again.)*
  - **★★ AND THE CUSTOMERS FORM NEVER CALLED `persistContact` AT ALL.** It wrote the edit into React state and closed. A customer's email — the thing `C353` needs to send them an invoice — lasted until the next reload, with no sign.
  - **★★★ I HAD ALREADY MET THIS BUG AND PATCHED ONE CALLER WITHOUT SEEING THE CLASS.** `C353`'s `resolveCustomerId` reads `existing.db_id || (isDbInvoiceId(existing.id) ? existing.id : null)` — an inline workaround for *"loaded contacts have no `db_id`"*, written three days ago. **A workaround at a call site is a finding about the writer, and I filed it as a detail of the caller.** It is `contactDbId` now, one resolver, and the inline copy is gone.
  - **THE FIX IS THREE THINGS, EACH PINNED:** the load mapping stamps `db_id: c.id` · `persistContact` resolves through `contactDbId` (a uuid `id` is a second net) and **a conflict no-op returns `ok: false`** with *"already exists — nothing was changed"* — the never-overwrite semantics stay for the enrichment race, but a caller wanting to say *saved* no longer can · **every form writes first and paints after** (`C238`): Vendors, Customers and both 1099 handlers await the verdict, keep the form open on a refusal, and say ✓ only on the record. Structure-pinned, because presence of `persistContact(` survives `if (false)`.
  - **★ TWO EXISTING TESTS WENT RED FOR THE RIGHT REASON.** One pinned the `C353` inline workaround verbatim (re-aimed at the resolver); one anchored on *the first* `const r = await persistContact(` in `VendorsView`, which is now the edit form rather than the merge block it meant (re-anchored inside `VendorMergeSuggestions`). **A test that pins a mechanism moves when the mechanism does — the failure to avoid is the assertion going with it.**

- **★★★ C360 — THE RECURRING SCREEN'S "POST NOW" NEVER WROTE THE ENTRY, AND ITS "+ ADD" NEVER SAVED THE RULE (2026-09-14). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** Found by auditing every *"✓"* toast for what gates it. `runRecurring` put the entry in React state, advanced `next_date` in React state, wrote an audit row *"Recurring posted"*, and said *"Posted ✓"* — **`bookToDb` was destructured and never called.** The `C288` `voidBook` shape exactly: the audit trail recording what never happened, on a screen a client reaches through Settings. And because the run was never recorded on the rule, a reload offered the same month again — *"Post now"* twice would have booked once. `addRecurring` was a `setRecurring`; the chat's `add_recurring` has persisted through a verified insert since `C112` (*"bot actions must stick"*), and the SCREEN's version never did. Both now write through the chat's paths (`createRecurring` IS `persistChatRecurring`; the run is recorded with `updateVerified` before *"Posted ✓"*), and a refused write says so and creates nothing.

- **★★ C358 — THE MATCHING SCREEN'S OPEN-ITEM PANELS DERIVED OPENNESS FROM `type` + `payment_status` — §9'S NAMED ANTI-PATTERN — AND DISAGREED WITH THE ENGINE BELOW THEM (2026-09-14). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS (one after its test was sharpened).** Found by rendering the reviewer screens with the upgraded fixture and reading the panels. `matchableOpenItems` (the one definition: a bill or invoice that ESTABLISHES a payable/receivable leg with no live settlement linked) has been the matcher's universe since `O73`; the panel headed *OPEN PAYABLES* above it still filtered `type==="expense" && payment_status!=="paid"`, so every direct-to-cash expense and every legacy bank line (`flattenJournalEntries` reads a null status as "unpaid") was listed as an open payable the engine would never propose a match for. The panels read the matcher's universe now, and the SIDE comes from the A/R leg rather than the `type` flag — a receivable whose flag drifted (the O73 root cause) is still a receivable. *(The upgraded fixture — offsets on every row, real role resolvers — is what made this visible; under the old one the panels had nothing to be wrong about.)*

Asked in free text now, the input the category question already uses.

The guard is for tokens; it does not pretend to judge layout.)*

- **★★★ C355 — A SUPPLIER PAID $145 IN AN ACCOUNT WE COULD NOT CLASSIFY GOT A "NEEDS A 1099" BADGE (2026-09-14). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** Found by rendering the Vendors tab with the populated fixture and reading the badges: Bluebonnet Linen Service (an LLC, $145, account `6180` — no role in the 1099 map) read *"Needs a 1099"* over the sentence *"was paid $0.00 for services this year, over the $600 floor"*. `verdictFor`'s floor check required `unknown <= 0`, its needs-info check required `reportable + unknown ≥ floor`, and a small unclassified amount satisfied NEITHER — so it fell through every guard to the ELIGIBLE default. **A false filing is the direction `C256` exists to refuse, and this was a false filing with a self-contradicting reason.** Under the floor is under the floor whatever we could not classify: the most that unclassified money could add is itself. A large unclassified amount is still a QUESTION, and real services over the floor from an LLC is still a filing — both pinned so the fix cannot go blind.

- **★ C352 — `O132`: THE STATEMENT IS ASKED FOR BEFORE THE OPENING BALANCE (2026-09-14). Every mechanism was already right — `deriveStatementOpening` prefers the printed figure, `shouldProposeOpening` raises the confirm card — and the checklist asked a person to invent the number one step before the document that answers it; a guess that disagreed with the statement then became a reconciliation mismatch the app rightly refuses to auto-adjust. - **★★ C351 — O82 CLIENT HALF: THE ADDRESS IN SETTINGS, THE HELD-MAIL LINE ON HOME, "BY EMAIL" ON DOCUMENTS, AND SEND INVOICE SENDS FROM THE APP WHEN IT CAN (2026-09-14). SEVEN MUTATIONS, SEVEN REDS (two after their tests were sharpened).** `src/lib/mailClient.js` is thin I/O around the SAME `_shared/mailChannel.js` the receiver uses — releasing a held message queues intake rows through the receiver's own `classifyAttachment`, so a file the receiver would have skipped is skipped here too. **Every surface degrades by SAYING which state it is in** (tables not there → *"we couldn't check"*, never *"no channel"* · no domain → the feature is named, no broken address · no channel → an admin sets one up, a member is told who can · a channel → the address, the allowed senders). `MAIL_DOMAIN` is a build-time constant, **null today**, so nothing on the live app changes until a domain exists — the screens render their "not switched on yet" sentence. Send Invoice's button **names its door** (*"Send Invoice →"* from the app, *"Send from my mail app →"* otherwise), sends only about a STORED invoice (`C350`'s id), and a refused send reads *"in your books but was NOT sent"*. Held messages: an owner/admin sees the sender and *Allow this sender* / *Ignore*; a member is told who can decide. **A mutation that let a release continue past a failed allow survived the first test** — the stub had no documents, so nothing would have been queued either way; the test now supplies a readable file and pins that it is NOT queued for a sender who was not allowed.

- **★★★ C350 — A SENT INVOICE DID NOT SURVIVE A RELOAD (2026-09-14). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** Found on the way to wiring `send-mail`'s `related_ar_invoice_id`: `loadAllData` has read `ar_invoices` on every load since the table existed, and **nothing has ever written it** — `persistSent` was a `setSentInvoices`. So Send Invoice booked a correct A/R entry and kept the invoice itself in React state until the next reload; after that the list was whatever the empty table said. **And the reload mapping dropped `journal_entry_id`**, so an invoice that DID reach the table (none had) would have had a *Mark paid* button that booked a SECOND revenue entry instead of clearing the A/R it had already booked — the "legacy" branch, reachable for every reloaded invoice. `arInvoiceRows` / `sentInvoiceFromRow` are one file in both directions (the `buildIntakeRow`/`fetchIntakeRows` shape); the header's totals are computed from the lines it stores, never copied from the draft; a failed persist is SAID as *"in your books, but the invoice itself won't be here after a reload"*, and *sent* is claimed only after the write. The O97 in-memory class on the surface the operator named first when asked what invoicing needs.

- **★ C348 — TWENTY `useState` HOOKS ON THE ROOT COMPONENT WERE WRITTEN AND NEVER READ (2026-09-14). TWO MUTATIONS, TWO REDS.** The `C347` guard, once it stopped counting destructures as readers, was pointed at state: a value referenced nowhere but its own declaration. **Twenty** — the whole QuickBooks-import set (`qboStep`, `qboData`, `qboMapping`, `qboPreview`, `qboProcessing`, `qboDragOver`), three reconciliation values, both narration pairs, `uploadProcessing`, `matchProcessing`, `checkRunMode`, `selectedPayments`, `glDrilldown`, `openingBalAsOfDate` — every one reset on every company switch, and `setUploadProcessing`/`setMatchProcessing` fired mid-pipeline, **each a root re-render of the whole application (`C297`'s cost) that could never change a pixel.** Their screens had long since moved to local state and nobody removed the originals. All twenty gone, with their setters and context exports; the guard now holds state to the same bar as functions.

BooksView never read `vendorFilter`; its one reader, `InvoicesView`, was deleted in `C335`. So from 2026-09-11 the door landed a person on every transaction in the company with nothing on screen saying a scope had been asked for. `filteredInvoices` — recomputed on every render, exposed on the context — was read by nothing.
  - **★★★ `tests/noUnreadDeclarations.test.js` PASSED IT, BECAUSE EVERY VIEW COPY-PASTES A ~300-NAME `const { … } = useERP()` DESTRUCTURE AND THE GUARD COUNTED THOSE LINES AS REFERENCES.** A name that appears in twenty destructures and no view body is exposed and unread exactly as if it were only on `erpCtx`. The guard strips those lists now and also covers `useMemo`/`useCallback` consts — **and its first honest run found FIVE more**: `applyRule`, `approveInvoice`, `rejectInvoice`, `requestInfo` and `glBreakdown`.

The 04:35 drive arithmetic (*"15 × 3 = 45, leaving 15 of 60"*) closes equally on 15 × 4 = 60 — **it never distinguished three from four**, and "measured" was overstated. Recorded, not re-derived.

Grouped and counted the way the Vendors tab does (`isVendorSpend`, credits subtract), one prior month is a coincidence not a usual, and a quiet month says *"no supplier charged noticeably more than usual"* rather than nothing. *(Who do I owe → Bills to pay; what am I spending on → By Category; both existed. An unreadable date yields null and the card keeps saying *"uploaded …"*, which is the sentence it can stand behind. **Bank statements got the same stamp in `C338`: their date is the period end, stated or inferred, written where the statement row is persisted — so *"find the January statement"* is answerable from the Documents tab whether or not the run linked it.**

- **★★★ C332 — `O138`: THE O117/O127 SPEC'S ONE CARD WAS HALF-WIRED — THE PAYABLE BOOKED, THE CARD NEVER APPEARED (2026-09-11). FIVE MUTATIONS, FIVE REDS.** Found by the `C331` sweep's cousin over `src/lib` exports: `countMismatchCopy` was exported, tested, and imported by nothing. Tracing why: `planInvoiceArrival` returns `BOOK_PAYABLE` + `reason: PERIOD_COUNT_MISMATCH` for the spec's step 4 (*"book the payable AND raise ONE card"*), and the caller in `App.jsx` reads only the action — a `BOOK_PAYABLE` with a reason falls through to ordinary booking and the reason is dropped on the floor. **The spec's §3 anti-vacuity criterion — *"it must raise exactly one card; if that card does not appear the fix is worse than the bug"* — was written, tested against the planner, and never true of the product.** The `·3a` shape with a third half: planner tested, copy tested, caller untested.
  - **★★ THE CARD IS DERIVED FROM THE LEDGER NOW, NOT RAISED AT ARRIVAL.** An open flat-fee bill in a period whose charges are all spoken for IS the condition — it survives a reload, resolves itself the moment the payment lands and clears the bill, needs no answer, and rides the existing anomaly lifecycle (`count:<vendor>:<YYYY-MM>` ends in a month, so the sign-off sweep can place it; MEDIUM, so it never blocks). **No imperative insert, no clarification-queue card that vanishes on reload.**
  - **★★★ AND MY FIRST VERSION DECIDED "OPEN" FROM `payment_status` — THE §9 ANTI-PATTERN BY NAME — AND THE HEALTHY-LEDGER SWEEP (`C291`) CAUGHT IT INSIDE A MINUTE.** `flattenJournalEntries` reads a null status as *"unpaid"*, so every legacy bank line would have been an open bill and the card would have fired on every flat-fee vendor's history. Openness is the OFFSET LEG being the company's A/P code, handed in by the caller; **without the code the rule is silent, never guessed** (`C309`). Pinned from both sides: a legacy row with no status is not a bill, and the caller passing `apCode` is a source guard because a mutation that dropped it survived everything else.
  - **★★ A SIDE-FINDING WORTH ITS OWN LINE: `runAnomalyDetection` CAPS ITS OUTPUT AT 25 CARDS, IN DETECTOR ORDER.** The flag-based draft flooded the healthy fixture with count cards and two UNRELATED detectors read zero — their cards were evicted, not suppressed. **A noisy early detector does not merely add cards; it silences the ones after it.** Recorded, not changed.
  - **★ THE `import_metadata` CONTRACT GUARD FIRED ON MY INLINE READ** of `invoice_attached` and was right to: one definition (`hasAttachedInvoice`) is imported instead of a second spelling.

- **★★ C331 — "DEFINED AND CALLED BY NOTHING" IS A GUARD NOW, AND ITS FIRST RUN FOUND ONE (2026-09-11).** `O137` was two functions with no caller behind two green tests; the natural question was *how many more*. A sweep over every arrow-declared const in `App.jsx` — referenced anywhere beyond its own line and the context export? — found exactly one: **`cashFromBanks`, exposed on the context and read by no component, computing Σ `bank_accounts.current_balance` — the one figure §12 says must never be shown as cash on hand.** Removed rather than left (`O96`/`C255`): a wired-looking function that produces the wrong cash total is worse than no function. `tests/noUnreadDeclarations.test.js` is the standing check; it refuses to pass vacuously (a planted helper is caught, and exposing it on the context is not a reader), and a mutation restoring the pre-`C329` shape — `priorExtraction` defined and uncalled — goes red by name.

- **★★ C330 — THE SLIDE-IN PANEL WAS NEVER UNDER THE JARGON BAR, AND THE OPERATOR'S SCREENSHOT WAS OF IT (2026-09-11). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS — AFTER TWO SURVIVED AND FIXED THE GUARD.** Every client VIEW passed `C315`'s check while the panel a client actually opens from Transactions read *GL account · Offset account · AI confidence · AI REASONING · Recode GL account*. `TransactionDetailPanel` is a component rendered INSIDE a client view, and the guard scanned views. It now reads *Category · Against · How sure we were · WHY IT WAS BOOKED THIS WAY · Change category*; the same *"GL Account"* column header on Transactions, Bills to pay, Customers, Vendors, the full-entry page and two Reports tables is *Category*; and the `O129` repair control reads *"Spread the cost over time"* rather than *"Set up depreciation"* — the guard found that one, not I.
  - **★★ TWO MUTATIONS SURVIVED AND BOTH WERE THE GUARD'S FAULT.** *(1)* Bare *"GL"* was not in `OWNER_JARGON_RE` — only *"GL code"* and *"general ledger"* — so the most common accounting abbreviation on the product walked past it; widened to `\bGL\b`, which immediately surfaced seven headers. *(2)* **The quoted-literal sweep paired `"` characters naively, so one `"` inside a `'…'` string earlier in the file shifted every later pair by one, and the panel's `"GL account"` label came out as the GAP between two labels.** It walks every string literal in order now — double, single and template. **A guard whose extractor is misaligned reports clean for the same reason a mutation that did not apply does.**
  - **★ AND A THIRD FIXED-WIDTH WINDOW OF THE DAY:** `depreciationRepair`'s check sliced 4,000 characters and reported the fix as a defect. Re-anchored on *CHANGE CATEGORY*, the control that follows the block.

- **★★★ C329 — `O137`: THE EXTRACTION CACHE WAS SHIPPED WITH BOTH ENDS CALLED BY NOTHING (2026-09-11). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** `C263` built `priorExtraction` and `storeExtraction` in `App.jsx`, migration `084` added the columns, two source guards pinned the functions' BODIES — and **neither function had a single call site.** `grep -c 'storeExtraction('` on the pre-fix file: **0**. So no document ever had a reading stored, no lookup could ever hit, and *"identical bytes re-uploaded reuse what we read"* was true of the code and false of the product for twelve days.
  - **★★★ FOUND BY LOOKING FOR SOMETHING ELSE.** The `O136` residue — 28 documents stored unlinked — could have been linked by SQL from each document's OWN stored reading (its vendor, amount and date are what booked the entry). I went to write that backfill and the column was null on every row, because the writer had never run. **A feature with no caller is the `C195(7)` shape at its purest: indistinguishable from one with nothing to do, and the guards that "covered" it read the function bodies — `·3a` on a single file.**
  - **WIRED:** the hash is computed once at the top of `processUploadItem`; the lookup runs before the classify call and a stored classification replaces it; a stored extraction skips the extract call and says so (`cacheHitCopy`, the `O128` rule); a fresh reading is stored against the durable document BEFORE the empty-check can return; non-invoice documents store their classification. **The coding call still runs on a hit** — the account is never reused, and a test asserts `code-invoices-batch` sits outside the cached branch.
  - **★★ THE NEW GUARD COUNTS CALL SITES, and its first version could not have failed.** I subtracted one for "the definition" — but the definitions read `const name = async (…)`, which never matches `name(`, so the count on the shipped code was already zero and my subtraction made the assertion `−1 ≥ 1`... which fails, correctly, but for an accident. Fixed to count plainly and verified against `HEAD`: **0 and 0.**
  - **★ AND A THIRD FIXED-WIDTH WINDOW BROKE.** `documentTypeContract`'s durable-first guard sliced 2,600 characters from a comment anchor; the lines this commit inserted pushed the guard out of the window and the test reported the fix as the defect. Re-anchored on the next call in the flow (`C260`'s rule, a third time).
  - **▶ WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO: it does not backfill the 28.** Their readings were never stored, so the SQL route is closed for them; the attach button remains the repair. **From this deploy on, a re-dropped invoice costs one AI call instead of three, and every document carries the reading that would let a future repair be a join rather than a click.**

The field is `null` now and the screen shows nothing — which is the truth about a payment. Demonstrated first (the filter returns the placeholder verbatim) and pinned by a tree walk; one mutation, two reds.

- **★★★ C324 — `O136`: THE DEDUPE STAMPED THE TYPE AND FORGOT THE LINK, SO EVERY INVOICE SINCE 2026-09-01 WAS IN THE LIBRARY AND INVISIBLE FROM ITS TRANSACTION (2026-09-11). SIX MUTATIONS, SIX REDS.** Operator, on the live app: *"when I click on a transaction they aren't showing the invoice — but I see all the invoices in the Documents tab."* Both halves of that sentence were exactly true.
  - **★★★ TWO `storeDocument` CALLS PER INVOICE FILE, AND THE SECOND ONE NEVER GOT TO SAY WHAT IT KNEW.** O97 stores the bytes BEFORE classification — type placeholder, link null. The invoice path then stores the SAME bytes again with the real type and the invoice they belong to, and C193's content-hash dedupe hands back the first row. `C300` taught that branch to stamp the type it now knew (`stampsOver`). **It never stamped `linked_invoice_id`**, so the row's link was decided at the only moment nobody had one. The relink after booking then ran `UPDATE … WHERE linked_invoice_id = <in-session id>` — matched nothing, moved nothing, warned nothing. **`findSourceDoc` on the detail panel found nothing, beside a library that plainly held the file.**
  - **★★ AND IT DATES FROM `C300`, WHICH IS WHY IT LOOKED NEW: before `O133` was fixed the durable-first insert always FAILED, so the invoice path's store was the FIRST store and inserted with its link.** Fixing the durable-first write made the dedupe branch the path every invoice takes — and that branch had only ever been taught half of what a second call knows. **A fix that makes a previously-dead branch live inherits every omission in it.**
  - **★★ THE MANUAL ATTACH BUTTON HAD THE SAME HOLE AND CLAIMED SUCCESS OVER IT.** *"Attach source document"* on a transaction stores the file with `linkedId = db_entry_id` — on a file already in the library that is the dedupe branch again, so it said *"Source document attached ✓"* and changed nothing. **The button the operator would reach for to repair this was itself broken by it.** It now links an existing file, which is also the one-at-a-time repair for the live residue.
  - **★ `dedupePatch` IS ONE DECISION FOR BOTH HALVES, and the link half is `linksOver` — MONOTONE LIKE `stampsOver`, only ever null → value.** A row that already backs an entry is never re-pointed: the same bytes dropped again and booked as a second entry (a genuine duplicate) must not move the document off the entry it already stands behind. Pinned, and mutation-killed.
  - **★★★ THE ·3a SHAPE, A FIFTH TIME — AND THIS ONE HAD THREE TESTED HALVES.** `stampsOver` was tested. `relinkDocsForInvoice` was tested. The matcher was tested. Each against its own fixture; **the SEQUENCE — store, store again, relink, reload, match — never was.** `tests/sourceDocLink.test.js` runs it, and one test restores the shipped dedupe (type only) and reproduces the operator's sentence exactly: **one row in the library, `type: invoice`, `linked_invoice_id: null`, and the transaction finds nothing.**
  - **★ THE FIRST MUTATION RUN DESTROYED THE FIX.** `git checkout` after each mutation restored the COMMITTED file — the fix was unstaged, so the first restore threw it away and five "mutations" then reported *"did not apply"* against the wrong baseline. **Stage the fix before mutating, and restore from the index.** Caught by the shasum-before-and-after check, which is the reason that check exists.
  - **★ AND A SIXTH MUTATION SURVIVED ON THE RECORDED BLIND SPOT:** `if (false)` around the checked write left a presence-matching guard green. Pinned as STRUCTURE — the non-empty-patch test must lead directly into the write — per `C238`/`C240`/`C248`.
  - **★★ C325 — THE REPAIR BUTTON IS GATED ON THE RECORD NOW (2026-09-11). THREE MUTATIONS, THREE REDS.** *"Source document attached ✓"* was shown unconditionally — on a persist failure, where `storeDocument` had **already raised its own error toast** and the ✓ landed on top of it, and on an already-stored file, where the dedupe branch changed nothing. **`C194`'s family: a success sentence gated on a click.** `storeDocument` now returns the in-session fallback when a link the caller ASKED FOR did not land on an existing row — callers passing no link (bank statements, the durable-first store) are untouched — and a failed stamp is REPORTED through `reportDocError`, the insert path's own convention, rather than left in the console. The panel shows ✓ only for a durable id. **This matters now more than usual: it is the button that repairs the 28 unlinked documents, and a false ✓ on it would mark the residue fixed while leaving it exactly as it was.**
  - **★★ C326 — AND THE OTHER DIRECTION: A DOCUMENT NOW SAYS WHICH ENTRY IT BECAME (2026-09-11). FOUR MUTATIONS, FOUR REDS.** *"Where did my receipt go?"* is the owner question `C313` named, and until now the Documents tab answered it with a filename. The card names the entry it produced (*Hill Country Milling Co. · $824.60*) and opens it — on the client seat, since `detail` is in its view set. **One resolver (`linkedEntryFor`) feeds the card's date, the card's door, and mirrors the detail panel's `findSourceDoc`** — `documentDate` had its own inline `.find`, which is how two screens come to point at different entries; a test asserts it no longer looks anything up itself. **The value of `C324` was never the link column; it is that the two screens can now reach each other through it.**
  - **▶ THE LIVE RESIDUE IS THE OPERATOR'S, AND IT IS SPLIT BY DATE.** `supabase/verify/O136_unlinked_documents.sql` counts unlinked invoice/receipt rows since 2026-09-01 and backfills ONLY those whose intake row (`C311`, 2026-09-10 on) recorded the entry. **Rows older than `C311` carry an empty `journal_entry_ids` and cannot be linked by SQL without guessing on amount and date** — which is precisely the silent mis-link this column must never carry. Those (the third drive's 28) are repaired one at a time from the transaction's own attach button.
